## Wave 2 private-feed publication covers the template package, ticket #54

Ticket #54's criterion "Use the existing central packaging, MinVer, CI validation and private-feed consumer path for new or changed Wave 2 packages", with the owner's decision of 2026-10-07: the publication workflow is fixed on the integration branch, and the real private-feed publication of the Wave 2 set runs on `main` with the merge of `feat/wave-2`, never from another branch. This package made no remote write: no push to the GitHub feed and no workflow run.

| Check | Evidence | Result |
|---|---|---|
| What the consumer step did with the template package before this change | Probe: `dotnet pack PouryaKit.slnx -c Release -o <feed>`, then a one-line `Consumer.csproj` with `<PackageReference Include="PouryaKit.Templates" Version="[0.1.0-preview.0.97]" />`, restored as the step restores (the feed folder named `GitHub` in the step's `NuGet.Config` shape, `--packages` and the HTTP and plugin caches isolated) | A silent success: exit 0 with no warning or error. `project.assets.json` lists `PouryaKit.Templates` as a package with an empty target entry, no compile, runtime or build asset; its 142 files are `content/` and metadata. The first `main` publication would not have failed: it would have proven that the nupkg downloads and nothing about its templates. |
| The changed step, run as written against a local feed | A scratch script outside the repository extracts the step's `run:` block from `.github/workflows/package-publication.yml`, replaces the GitHub feed URL with an empty local folder and runs it with `bash -c` from the checkout, with the step's variables (`PACKAGES_DIR`, `RUNNER_TEMP`, `GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`, a stand-in `GITHUB_TOKEN` and `NuGetPackageSourceCredentials_GitHub`) | On commit `071625d`: exit 0 in 43 s with the packages and `manifest.json` of a `validate.py` run (commit `1dde46f`, version `0.1.0-preview.0.109`, 21 packages), and exit 0 in 42 s with `dotnet pack` output at `0.1.0-preview.0.97`. The step's own `dotnet nuget push` filled the empty folder with all 21 nupkgs; the library consumer restored the 20 library packages; `PouryaKit.Templates` installed from that folder, which the hive's `packages.json` records as its `NuGetSource`; `pouryakit-modular-monolith` and `pouryakit-monolith` were each generated as `Acme.Shop` and as `acme-shop` and restored (7 and 5 projects), each generated Host's closure holding its Kit packages at the manifest's version only. The step summary named the library consumer, the install and the four generated solutions. |
| The template package must come from the feed | The same run with `PouryaKit.Templates` left out of the manifest, so the push never reaches the feed with it | Exit 103 at `dotnet new install`: `PouryaKit.Templates@0.1.0-preview.0.97 is not found in NuGet feeds` naming the GitHub stand-in alone. |
| The install reads the GitHub source alone | That control with the step's `dotnet nuget disable source nuget.org` line removed | The install also searched `https://api.nuget.org/v3/index.json`. `dotnet new install` in SDK 10.0.401 ignores package source mapping: `NuGetApiPackageManager.LoadNuGetSources` (`Microsoft.TemplateEngine.Edge`) takes the enabled sources of the working folder's `NuGet.Config`, adds each `--add-source` not already there, and the first source with the exact version wins. |
| Workflow checks | `python3 -c "import yaml…"` on both publication workflows, `actionlint` with `shellcheck`, `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests/releases/publication -p 'test_*.py'`, `python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts/packaging -p 'test_*.py'`, `python3 .agents/scripts/check-ai-setup.py` | All passed; the publication checks still extract and run the artifact-verification step unchanged. *Since 2026-10-10 ([ticket #90](#pre-wave-3-capability-docs-adr-0006-and-repository-hygiene-ticket-90)) the test is `scripts/ci/test_publication.py`, run by `build-and-test`'s discovery of `scripts/ci`.* |

Not proven locally: the credentialed install from GitHub Packages. `NuGetPackageSourceCredentials_GitHub` reaches only a source named `GitHub` in a configuration file (NuGet's `PackageSourceProvider` reads `NuGetPackageSourceCredentials_<name>`), which is why the install runs beside the copied `NuGet.Config`; an `--add-source` URL would carry no credentials. The first `main` publication after the merge of `feat/wave-2` is that proof. *It ran on 2026-10-09 and passed: [Wave 2 hosted publication from main](#wave-2-hosted-publication-from-main).* The generated solutions are restored there, not built or tested: `validate.py`'s template consumers build them, run their smoke tests and publish their Hosts from the produced packages.

## Wave 2 pipeline contracts only the Kit implements, ticket #54

Ticket #54's acceptance criteria "Structure/composition checks" and "Reconcile … applicable ADR amendments", for the
owner's decision of 2026-10-07 (D4): spec #33's "Applications cannot register arbitrary pipeline steps" is enforced by a
structure test, and ADR 0002 says what holds ([its #54 amendment](adr/0002-no-mediator-dependency.md)). No Kit code
changed.

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| An application type that implements a contract the Kit fills for its pipeline is rejected, naming the type, the contract and the assembly's file | `PipelineContractTests.An_application_type_that_implements_a_pipeline_contract_is_rejected` | The negative case is `Offending.OwnQueryCache`, an `IQueryCache`. Red once with the type no longer implementing `IQueryCache`, and with the rule's contract list empty |
| No application code of the Proving Sample implements one: both Hosts and every project they reference that is no Kit package; the Kit's packages hold each one's implementations | `PipelineContractTests.No_module_or_host_implements_a_pipeline_contract_and_the_kit_implements_each` | The contracts: `IDispatcher`, `IGatewayProcessor<TModule>`, `IUnitOfWork`, `IValidationStep<T>`, `IReadingStateGuard`, `IUniqueConstraintReader`, `IIdempotencyStore`, `IQueryCache`, `IUnitOfWorkOutbox`, `IOutbox`, `IIntegrationEventOutbox`, `IDelayedCommandOutbox`, `ISnapshotMappers`, `IReceipts`; `IDispatcher` has two Kit implementations, Cqrs's and Observability's decorator. Red with the open-generic match off (`FluentValidationStep<T>` went unseen), with the list empty, with `IDispatcher` and `IGatewayProcessor<TModule>` taken off it, and with `IUniqueConstraintReader` taken off it (Persistence.Postgres's `PostgresUniqueConstraintReader` missing). `IAuthorizer<T>` and the handler contracts stay the application's |

## Wave 2 test gaps of the completeness audit and the two-database probe, ticket #54

Ticket #54's criteria "Each preceding ticket retains its own tests, runtime proof and review obligations" and
"Summarize real PostgreSQL … evidence". The completeness audit of 2026-10-07 named four statements of spec #33 that no
test exercised; the owner asked for the one unverified point of the messaging guarantee, a Module's DbContext and the
message store on different databases, to be proven before the Wave closes. No Kit code changed.

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| An idempotent command on PUT and on DELETE requires the `Idempotency-Key`: without it, 400 as a problem response with the RFC 9110 type, the command not run; with it, a repeat answers the first status and body and runs, writes and sends nothing | `IdempotencyTests.An_idempotent_put_or_delete_without_a_key_answers_400_and_with_one_replays_the_first_response` (PUT, DELETE) | No command of the Proving Sample opts in on either method: `RenameProduct` (PUT) does not opt in, and Catalog maps nothing on DELETE; none was opted in for the test. The Stub's `[Idempotent]` `CountNoteCommand` is mapped on both (`CountedNoteEndpoint`). POST keeps its tests above (#42). Red once with the key read only on POST: the repeat ran the command again and stored a second Notification, while the POST test stayed green |
| A delayed command's delivery Operation is a child of the Operation that scheduled it, in its trace, with the upstream tracestate, after the store held it until due; the command it runs is that Operation's child | `ObservabilityTests.A_delayed_command_runs_in_the_trace_of_the_operation_that_scheduled_it_with_its_tracestate` | Billing's `OpenQuote` with a 300 ms expiry. A scheduled envelope is not sent at the flush: the store promotes it when due and hands it out, so the parent-id and the Kit's tracestate header come back from the stored envelope. Red once with `DelayedCommandDelivery` ignoring the restored parent: the delivery started a trace of its own |
| The rate limit's partition names the Actor's kind and id: a person who bears the storefront's Actor id is not one of its anonymous visitors (*the Stub's public endpoint's since #116, 2026-10-16*) | `WebDefaultsTests.A_person_named_as_a_public_endpoints_actor_has_a_partition_apart_from_its_anonymous_callers` | The key is `{Kind}:{Id}` in `ActorRateLimiting`; [web.md](../.agents/skills/pourya-kit/references/web.md) states the partition by Actor, not the string. The Host shape #62 left: authentication registered, `UseAuthentication()` ahead of the limiter. Red once with the key on the id alone: the person was refused with 429 |
| A trusted peer's context headers that cannot be read resolve no Actor: the limiter counts the call in the one partition of requests no Actor resolves for, and the endpoint answers 400; neither the peer's own partition nor the claimed Actor's moves | `WebDefaultsTests.A_trusted_peers_unreadable_context_headers_count_in_the_partition_no_actor_resolves_for_and_answer_400` | A correlation id that is not a Guid. Without the branch the first call answers 400 too, since the `BadHttpRequestException` escaping the limiter reaches the exception handler, so the status alone cannot tell; the second call does. Red once with the catch off: the second answered 400 instead of 429 |
| An operation whose policy names its own scheme requires that scheme in the OpenAPI document, not the Host's default one | `OpenApiTests.An_operation_whose_policy_names_a_scheme_requires_that_scheme_and_not_the_default_one` | The fixture's default scheme is its test scheme, which the document cannot state; the Host registers JWT bearer too. Only the endpoint whose `AuthorizeAttribute` names `Bearer` carries `[{"Bearer":[]}]`. Red once with the policy's schemes ignored: no operation carried a requirement |

### A Module's DbContext and the message store on two databases (a recorded probe)

The rule is `UsePouryaKitWolverine`'s: the store is the database of its connection string, "which every Module's
DbContext must also use" ([messaging.md](../.agents/skills/pourya-kit/references/messaging.md#declaring-handlers-and-running-deliveries)). The
probe `MessageStoreDatabaseTests` (commit `3861dc9`, removed in `e71f92e`) composed a Host without Modules on the
fixture's PostgreSQL 18.6 container, WolverineFx 6.46.0: `UsePouryaKitPostgres<StubDbContext>(connectionName: "app", schema:
"stub")` on one new database, `UsePouryaKitWolverine(connectionName: "messages")` on a second, the Stub's Notification
Handlers, and `Publish` and `Subscribe` of `NoteSharedV1`. One command writes a note whose Domain Event the
`ShareNoteNotificationHandler` publishes `NoteSharedV1` for; another writes a note and sends nothing. It asserts nothing;
it wrote what follows.

| Composition | What happened |
|---|---|
| The Module's database fresh, holding no store tables | The Host was built and started; no composition or startup check refused it. The first command threw `Npgsql.PostgresException` `42P01: relation "wolverine.wolverine_incoming_envelopes" does not exist`, from `EfCoreEnvelopeTransaction.PersistIncomingAsync`, called by `WolverineOutbox.WriteAsync` inside `EfUnitOfWork.RunAsync`: Wolverine writes the envelope on the DbContext's own connection and transaction, in the store's schema, so in the Module's database; it opens no connection to the store. The Unit of Work's transaction rolled back: no note in the Module's database, no row in the store's incoming, outgoing or dead-letter tables, no Notification Handler or Integration Event Handler run, and the dispatcher logged `WriteNoteRaisingCommand threw` at Error. The command that sends nothing committed. The mistake surfaces at the first Operation that writes to the Outbox, atomically, not when the Host is built. |
| The Module's database holding the store's tables, left by a Host that used it as its store | The Outbox write went into that copy and committed with the note. The flush after the commit handed both envelopes to the Host's local queues in memory, so the Notification Handler and the Integration Event Handler each ran once, their Receipts and the publication record in `stub.pouryakit_receipts`. Both envelope rows stayed `Incoming`, owned by node 1, in tables the Host's store never reads; the store's own tables stayed empty; nothing was logged at Warning or above but Wolverine's two handler-discovery notices. A stored retry or a recovery after a crash would read the store, which holds none of them (inferred, not run): the Outbox's durability is gone without a word. For the owner's decision, with a candidate check: compare the store's connection string with each `UsePouryaKitPostgres` connection when the Host is built. |

No permanent test: the rule is a documented deployment rule; that the Outbox write joins the Unit of Work's
transaction is pinned by the commit-timestamp tests (`NotificationTests`, `IntegrationEventTests`), which a write on
another connection would turn red; and a test of the fresh-database row would pin the absence of a check.

`dotnet test --project tests/ModularMonolith.Tests/ModularMonolith.Tests.csproj` on this package's tree, merged with
`feat/wave-2` at `b7f0dce`, printed 504 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. No `validate.py` run: the package changes tests and
this record only.

## Wave 2 the message store and every DbContext share one database, ticket #63

Ticket #63, from the two-database probe above: the message store's connection and every connection a Module's or the
application's DbContext uses (`UsePouryaKitPostgres`) must name one database, and a Host that breaks the rule fails when it is
built instead of writing its Outbox where the store never reads it. The probe's two observations stay as recorded. One
composition check in the shape of #62's connection-string checks, `MessageStoreDatabaseCheck` of
`PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine.EFCore`, registered by `UsePouryaKitWolverine(connectionName)`, compares the string
Wolverine was given with the connection EF Core gives each DbContext, whatever configured it, on its hosts, each with its
port, and its database, as Npgsql reads them; it opens no connection. A string that names neither a database nor a user
leaves the database to the client's user, so it fails the build too. Its limit: host names are not resolved
(`localhost` and `127.0.0.1` differ). The DbContexts' connections reach it through one record,
`DbContextConnection` of `PouryaKit.Persistence.EFCore`, moved there from #62's `PostgresConnection` so that both
checks read it; no reference was added ([ADR 0004](adr/0004-commit-through-wolverine-ef-outbox.md)'s #63 amendment).

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| A DbContext whose connection string names another database than the store's, by its name, its port or its host, fails the Host when it is built, one line naming both connection names, both databases and the fix | `MessageStoreDatabaseTests.A_dbcontext_on_another_database_than_the_message_store_fails_the_host_when_built_naming_both` (database, port, host) | The probe's composition: a Production Host without Modules, the Stub's DbContext as its Unit of Work on `app`, the store on `messages`, both derived from the fixture's PostgreSQL. Neither database is created: nothing connects while the Host is built, so a check that connected would have failed differently. Red once with the check off: all three Hosts built, as the probe's did; and with hosts and ports ignored: the port and host rows built |
| Two connection names for one database pass, however the strings are written | `MessageStoreDatabaseTests.A_dbcontext_and_the_message_store_on_one_database_under_two_connection_names_build` | The store's string says `Server` for `Host`, puts the port inside the host, writes the host in upper case and adds an `Application Name`. Red once with the strings compared as written: the Host failed to build |
| A string read as Npgsql reads it: one that names no database names its user's, and a Unix socket's directory keeps its case and takes the separate port; so two users without a database, two socket directories that differ in case, or one directory with two ports fail the build | `MessageStoreDatabaseTests.A_dbcontext_on_another_database_as_npgsql_reads_the_strings_fails_the_host_when_built_naming_both` (users, socket case, socket ports) | Review findings F1 and F3 of `99a6d0e`. Red once with the database read from `Database` alone: the two users built; and with the socket detection off: the two socket directories built |
| A database named as the other side's user and a port written inside the host with a leading zero name one database and build | `MessageStoreDatabaseTests.Two_strings_npgsql_reads_as_one_database_build` (the user's database, `db:05432` against `Port=5432`) | Review findings F1 and F4. Red once with the port inside the host kept as text: `db:05432` failed, and so did the Fact above, whose port is written inside its host |
| A DbContext's or the store's string that names neither a database nor a user fails the build, one line naming that connection and the fix (`Database`, or at least `Username`) | `MessageStoreDatabaseTests.A_dbcontext_whose_string_names_neither_a_database_nor_a_user_fails_the_host_when_built_naming_its_connection`, `…A_message_store_whose_string_names_neither_a_database_nor_a_user_fails_the_host_when_built_naming_its_connection` | Finding F1 of the second review (`38c0dc1`): Npgsql then takes the user from `PGUSER`, the GSS principal or the OS user, and the server uses its name as the database, which no string shows; it had been a wildcard. Red once with the unknown-database lines switched off: both Hosts built |
| A `ConfigureDbContext` after `UsePouryaKitPostgres` that points the DbContext at another database fails the build, naming that database | `MessageStoreDatabaseTests.A_dbcontext_that_configure_db_context_moves_to_another_database_fails_the_host_when_built_naming_it` | Review finding F2: the check reads the connection EF Core gives the DbContext. Red once with the configuration string of the recorded name read instead: the Host built |
| A DbContext moved onto an Npgsql data source is checked on the source's database: on another database it fails the build naming it, the string `UsePouryaKitPostgres` wrote cleared first; on the store's it builds, with that string left in place naming another | `MessageStoreDatabaseTests.A_dbcontext_moved_onto_a_data_source_on_another_database_fails_the_host_when_built_naming_it`, `…A_dbcontext_moved_onto_a_data_source_on_the_message_stores_database_builds_whatever_string_it_left` | Finding F5 of the second review (`38c0dc1`): EFCore.PG 10's `UseNpgsql(dataSource)` sets only the provider's data source and leaves the options' string. The check reads `Database.GetDbConnection().ConnectionString` of the DbContext created in its own scope, not opened. Red once with the options' string read back (`RelationalOptionsExtension`, as at `38c0dc1`): the first Host built and the second failed on the stale string |
| A DbContext EF Core refuses when the check constructs it, a named form the configuration lacks or a model error, fails the build with EF Core's own exception, not a line of the check's | `MessageStoreDatabaseTests.A_dbcontext_on_a_named_string_the_configuration_lacks_fails_the_host_when_built_with_ef_cores_exception`, `…A_dbcontext_whose_model_ef_core_refuses_fails_the_host_when_built_with_ef_cores_exception` | Re-score finding R6 of `f58a082`: the DbContext's constructor builds its connection and its model, both failing as `InvalidOperationException`, so the check, which had quoted either under a line asking for a connection, no longer catches them; EF Core's message names the cause ("A named connection string was used, but the name … was not found", "The entity type 'Keyless' requires a primary key to be defined"). A DbContext with no string, connection or data source at all is given an unopened connection with an empty string by Npgsql's provider, which names neither a database nor a user, so it fails with that line. Red once with the construction wrapped back in a catch that turns it into a check line: both Hosts failed with the check's line |
| One record of each DbContext's connection feeds both checks | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_connection_string_the_configuration_lacks_or_leaves_blank_fails_the_host_when_built_naming_its_key`; the rows above | Red once with `UsePouryaKitPostgres` registering no `DbContextConnection`: the missing-string line was gone and the three Hosts of the first row built. A missing or blank DbContext string stays `PostgresConnectionsCheck`'s line alone. |

## Wave 2 documentation and AI guidance reconciled, ticket #54

Ticket #54's criterion "Reconcile Capability documentation, C4 diagrams, applicable ADR amendments and the Kit's
skill/rules/agent guidance with delivered behavior. Preserve JWT token creation as an excluded recommendation and record
owner additions accurately", the completeness audit of 2026-10-07 and the owner's decision of 2026-10-07 on the monolith:
keep the single-Module monolith and make the templates' roles and its growth path explicit. Documentation and AI
guidance only: no Kit code changed, and in template content only the two `template.json` descriptions and one comment of
the generated smoke test.

| What | Where | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| The templates' roles: the monolith is the single-Module start that grows into the modular monolith by adding `PouryaKit.Modularity`; the modular monolith is several Modules, each reached through its Gateway | Both `template.json` descriptions; [templates.md](../.agents/skills/pourya-kit/references/templates.md), "Growing the monolith into a modular monolith"; [the architecture doc](architecture/README.md)'s Proving Sample view, which gains the template package | Both templates generated with `-n Acme.Shop` from the package packed at this tree: twelve files differ and the rest are the same files; nine change when Catalog becomes a Module (`Directory.Packages.props`, `Catalog.csproj`, `CatalogModule.cs`, the five endpoints, `Program.cs`), three by Billing and Inventory (the solution, the Host's csproj, `appsettings.json`); the modular monolith adds Billing, Inventory, `CatalogProducts.cs` and `CrossModuleTests.cs`. The two traps of the path, a declaration without its Module and a `ConfigureContainer` of the Host's own, are stated as closed by #61 and #62. `TemplateTests` passes. |
| JWT token creation is an excluded recommendation, not a deliverable | [security.md](../.agents/skills/pourya-kit/references/security.md), "JWT bearer authentication" | Spec #33's Implementation Decisions keep it a recommendation, not an accepted owner decision or a Wave 2 deliverable, and plan I.3 refuses token issuance; no Kit package issues a token (`PouryaKit.Security.JwtBearer` validates and normalizes only, #39). |
| Owner additions recorded as additions | Plan I.3: the Paging row (allow-listed filters, #35), the Security row (PBKDF2, #40) and, added now, the HttpClient context handler row (outgoing HTTP retry without Aspire, #48) | Each named as the owner's addition in spec #33, as its Implementation Decisions ask, not as text the checked plan held. |
| The plan's catalogue | Plan I.3's One JSON setup (#34) and Integration Event versioning (#49) rows gain their Built notes, the latter its Glossary words ("a subscription to v1"); the Health row's proof is two compositions in Wave 2; I.6's ADR 0002 slots and ADR 0006 RabbitMQ answer are marked resolved | Section C's Cqrs, Modularity, Persistence.Postgres and Security.JwtBearer rows checked against each csproj: unchanged. |
| ADRs keep their records; dated notes only | 0006: the #50 record's `StartupCheck.cs` is `DurableQueuesCheck.cs` since #62, and its inferred point tested in #51; 0008: #36's note of 2026-10-07 under an amendment heading of its own, word for word; 0009: the Outbox-write placement #47 showed red | ADR 0004's #58 amendment checked against the four Hosts' csproj files and `Program.cs`: unchanged. |
| The architecture doc and the Kit's skill follow #58 to #63 | The Wolverine package's checks named at their own moments (composition checks, the routing checks as Wolverine starts, `DurableQueuesCheck` once it has started) instead of "the startup checks"; `AddPouryaKitEndpoints`, `AddPouryaKitValidation`, `AddPouryaKitPermissions` and `AddPouryaKitDapper` with the Module's assembly inferred (#61); the Glossary's words ("subscription", "recurring command"); `SKILL.md`'s structure rules gain the pipeline-contract rule and its triggers the check terms; `README.md`'s status covers #59, #60 and #63; `docs/private-packages.md` says what a complete version's feed check is now and that the template install's hosted proof is the first `main` publication | The Mermaid views parse (`mermaid.parse` on each block, with a mutated block refused); every relative link and anchor of the changed files resolves. |
| AI guidance | `docs/agents/brief-template.md`'s docs-sync set gains `docs/adr/*.md` and `src/PouryaKit.Templates/content/**`; `.claude/rules/kit-code.md`'s Stack line names the generated templates' smoke tests; `docs/agents/ai-setup.md`'s Known gaps record #53's Codex timeout and `worktree.baseRef: head` as confirmed | `python3 .agents/scripts/check-ai-setup.py`: 0 failures. `docs/agents/running-a-spec.md` names no start-time check in any step, so it is unchanged. |

A recorded limitation, from the #62 re-score: a Host made of `AddPouryaKitWeb` alone, with no Cqrs registration,
registers no check runner. The runner comes with the dispatcher's registration (`AddDispatcher` in
`src/PouryaKit.Cqrs/CqrsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs`), which only `AddPouryaKitCqrs` and
`AddPouryaKitCompositionChecks` make, so such a Host starts with its startup checks unrun, `UnmappedEndpointsCheck`
included. It runs nothing degraded: each Module route group begins the request's Operation
(`src/PouryaKit.Web/Endpoints/WebEndpointRouteBuilderExtensions.cs`), and `BeginOperation` resolves the `Operation` only that
registration adds, so the Host fails at its first request instead.

## Wave 2 final structural review

The Wave 2 final structural review of 2026-10-08 (tip `74e873b`; two independent legs, Claude and Codex, read-only)
found defects in closed tickets. By the owner's instruction each is fixed at its root, with runtime proof, and no ticket
is reopened; the fixes ran as four packages on disjoint files, each recorded under its own heading here.

### Package A: Web, Security and two doc lines

| Finding | Fix | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 (high, both legs): a trusted peer's HTTP continuation was built with the public constructor, so it was never `IsTrustedContinuation` and the permission check authorized the continued Actor again, against ADR 0007's #57 amendment (#48, #57) | `OperationContextHttpHeaders.Continued` returns `OperationContext.RestoreTrusted(...)`; it is reached only after `HttpCallerContext` verified the authenticated trusted peer, an order left unchanged | `TrustedPeerTests.A_trusted_peer_continues_into_a_permission_protected_command_its_actor_holds_no_permission_for_and_an_untrusted_caller_is_refused`; `TrustedRestoreTests.The_packages_cqrs_opens_to_use_no_internal_member_of_it_but_the_trusted_restore` | HTTP seam on the Proving Sample's Host: the peer, with no permission and continuing Ada, who holds none either, removes a product through the Stub's `RemoveProductsCommand` endpoint, recorded as removed by Ada; the same headers from Grace, an untrusted caller, answer 403 `Permission.Denied` and nothing changes. The structure test now requires Web's call beside delivery's. Red once with the public constructor back: the peer answered 403, and the structure test found no Web call. [ADR 0007](adr/0007-operation-context-set-by-the-scope-opener.md) has a dated note. |
| R5 (medium, Codex): `RequireIdempotencyKey()` passed an endpoint mapped for no method, without method metadata or with an empty list, which answers every method, GET included (#62) | The `Finally` hook refuses absent and empty method metadata when the endpoint is built, naming the fix (map the command on POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE); the safe-method refusal and its message are unchanged | `MisconfiguredHostTests.An_endpoint_that_requires_an_idempotency_key_but_names_no_method_fails_the_host_at_start` (both shapes); `MisconfiguredHostTests.An_endpoint_that_requires_an_idempotency_key_on_a_get_fails_the_host_at_start`; `IdempotencyTests` | Red once with the new refusal off: both Hosts started. A list of methods none of which is safe passes, an extension method included, as `HttpReplay.SafeMethods` reads safety. [ADR 0002](adr/0002-no-mediator-dependency.md)'s #62 exception is replaced by a dated note. |
| R8 (low, Codex): `observability.md` said the service defaults recipe exports Wolverine's spans (#47) | The recipe registers the application's source and `OperationTelemetry.SourceName`; `.AddSource("Wolverine")` is opt-in, as `host-recipes.md` already said | Docs only | |
| R6 (low, Claude): `Actor.System`'s doc said "scheduled job", a Glossary avoid-word | "a recurring command's occurrence" | Docs only | |

The received-type case gains a subscription, or the new line below comes first and no name is declared. Two tests of plain Hosts that subscribe without one, `IntegrationEventVersionTests.Building_fails_when_wolverine_would_route_one_message_type_to_the_subscriptions_of_another` and the received-type case above, now find their line among two. |

### Package C: a recurring command's schedule survives a dead-lettered occurrence

| Finding | Fix | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| R2 (medium, Claude): a failed occurrence rolled back the next occurrence it had stored with its effect, so once its stored retries were spent it was dead-lettered and nothing stored a next one; the declaration stopped until a replay or a Host start, against plan I.3's "durable replacement for in-memory Quartz stores" and the Glossary's "every occurrence of a cadence" (#45) | `DelayedCommandDelivery` recognises the attempt whose failure sets the occurrence aside, the one numbered beyond `PouryaKitMessagingOptions.RetryDelays` (Wolverine counts an attempt before the handler runs, then takes the retry policy's slot of that number), and before it rethrows stores the next occurrence after now in an Operation of its own unless one waits, through `RecurringCommandStart`'s query and Operation; a successful occurrence still stores the next one in its own commit | `RecurringCommandTests.An_occurrence_set_aside_as_a_dead_letter_stores_the_next_occurrence_which_runs`; `RecurringCommandTests.An_occurrence_that_succeeds_on_its_last_attempt_leaves_only_the_next_occurrence_its_commit_stored` | Inventory's `ReconcileStock` at a one-second cadence, on a database of its own. The first: `DeliveryProbes.FailAfterReceipt` fails every attempt until the first dead letter appears, and no Host starts again and nothing is replayed; each occurrence set aside, read from its dead letter's `DelayedCommandHeaders.Occurrence` header, had three attempts and left no reconciliation or Receipt, and every reconciliation is of a later occurrence, under the system Actor, with its Receipt. Red once with the dead-letter branch off: no reconciliation within 15 s. The second: `FailAfterReceipt` gains a count and fails the first two deliveries, and the store is read while the occurrence's commit is held: one occurrence waits, committed with the reconciliation. Red once with the attempt recognised one too early (`>=`), and once with every failed attempt storing one: two waited. [ADR 0006](adr/0006-independent-delivery-through-a-routed-queue-per-subscription.md)'s #45 amendment has a dated note citing Wolverine's source. |

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch` on package C's commit `03b724c`, merged with `feat/wave-2` at
`2c3b617` (before this paragraph was added), printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof`
at version `0.1.0-preview.0.117`, with the Release runtime suite at 786 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. ModularMonolith.Tests
passed first: 525 passed; ArchitectureTests 98 passed. Before the merge, the same check on `b803a68` had passed at
`0.1.0-preview.0.114`, with 783 passed.

### Package D: every Unit of Work's DbContext checked against the store; the negative delivery assertion sees broker envelopes

Findings 2 (high) and 6 (medium). `AddPouryaKitEfCore<TDbContext>(connectionName = null)` records the DbContext's
`DbContextConnection` once, where the Outbox attaches, with the connection name `UsePouryaKitPostgres` passes it and none for a
DbContext the application registered itself; `MessageStoreDatabaseCheck` checks every record and `PostgresConnectionsCheck`
only the named ones. `AssertNothingSentAsync` also fails on an envelope Wolverine handed to a sending agent for a broker
while the Operation ran, read from Wolverine's `wolverine-messages-sent` counter on the Host runtime's own `Meter` (the
store deletes a sent envelope), and on one still stored for a broker afterwards (a latched sender hands nothing out).

| Guarantee | Test | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| A DbContext the application registered with `AddDbContext` on another database, made a Unit of Work with `AddPouryaKitEfCore`, fails the build naming it, beside a store on the fixture's database | `MessageStoreDatabaseTests.A_dbcontext_the_application_registered_on_another_database_fails_the_host_when_built_naming_it` | With `AddPouryaKitEfCore` recording only a named DbContext, the Host built. |
| A record with no name is not the named-connection check's: such a DbContext on the store's database builds beside `UsePouryaKitPostgres` | `MessageStoreDatabaseTests.A_dbcontext_the_application_registered_on_the_message_stores_database_builds_beside_use_pouryakit_postgres` | With `PostgresConnectionsCheck` reading the record's null name, the build failed on a missing `ConnectionStrings:` line. |
| An outgoing-only publication fails `AssertNothingSentAsync`: a Snapshot only Inventory.Host subscribes to, sent and deleted before the Operation returns, and with its sender paused (`SendingAgent.PauseAsync`, as Wolverine latches it while the broker is unavailable) | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.Assert_nothing_sent_fails_on_a_snapshot_sent_only_to_the_broker_once_sent_and_while_it_waits_for_the_broker` | With the counter's listener not started, the first assertion passed; with the stored outgoing envelopes not read, the second passed. |

version `0.1.0-preview.0.120`.

Fix round (dual review of `ce38cc7`, findings F1 to F3). F1 (high): a name passed to `AddPouryaKitEfCore<TDbContext>(connectionName)`
without `UsePouryaKitPostgres` escaped both checks, because only `UsePouryaKitPostgres` registered the missing-string check and
`MessageStoreDatabaseCheck` leaves a missing named string to it. The check moved to where the records are made: Persistence.EFCore's
`DbContextConnectionsCheck`, registered by `AddPouryaKitEfCore` with `TryAddEnumerable`, with `PostgresConnectionsCheck`'s
message text (the record carries no origin, so the line still names `UsePouryaKitPostgres`); `PostgresConnectionsCheck` is deleted.
F2 (medium): a name now replaces an earlier record of the DbContext without one, and a second, different name throws
`InvalidOperationException` at registration naming both. F3 (low): `modules.md`'s store-database bullet names the line's
parts for each case.

| Guarantee | Test | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| A connection name `AddPouryaKitEfCore` records without `UsePouryaKitPostgres`, which the configuration lacks, fails the build naming `ConnectionStrings:unset`, beside a store on another database | `MessageStoreDatabaseTests.A_connection_name_add_pouryakit_ef_core_records_and_the_configuration_lacks_fails_the_host_when_built_naming_its_key` | With `DbContextConnectionsCheck` not registered, the Host built; `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_connection_string_the_configuration_lacks_or_leaves_blank_fails_the_host_when_built_naming_its_key` failed too, so the one check covers `UsePouryaKitPostgres`'s names. |
| `AddDbContext`, `AddPouryaKitEfCore<T>()`, then `UsePouryaKitPostgres<T>("missing", …)` fails the build naming `ConnectionStrings:missing` | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_connection_string_named_after_an_unnamed_record_of_the_dbcontext_fails_the_host_when_built_naming_its_key` | With the unnamed record kept, the Host built. |
| A second, different connection name for one DbContext throws at registration, naming both | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_dbcontext_given_a_second_connection_string_throws_naming_both` | With the throw removed, the second `UsePouryaKitPostgres` returned. |

`SharedStoreSchemaTests` 1, `ModularMonolith.Tests` 537, `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 98 (`PackageGraphTests` 25), all
passed; `check-ai-setup.py` 0 failures.

Second full review (`9a5b405`, findings F4 to F6). F4 (medium): the two-names throw compared connection names ordinally,
so `"app"` and `"APP"`, one configuration key, threw at registration; `AddPouryaKitEfCore` now compares them ignoring case,
keeps the first spelling and throws only on names that differ beyond case, and `DbContextConnectionsCheck` groups its lines

string missing, which the Host's configuration does not set (ConnectionStrings:missing), so every Operation that opens it
would fail. Set ConnectionStrings:missing, or name a connection the Host configures where StubDbContext is registered.`

## Wave 2 close-out, ticket #54

The final `validate.py --no-fetch` run of the Wave, on `930d8ba` (the `feat/wave-2` tip after the final structural review's four fix packages and their re-scores), is the package record for every Wave 2 section without a PASS line of its own and for every PASS line older than its section's last source change (the top note): `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof; version 0.1.0-preview.0.123`. The final structural review (two independent legs over the whole tree at `74e873b`), its dispositions and its four fix packages are recorded in the section above and in #54's closing comment; the owner's two open decisions (the `PouryaKit` prefix for `UsePostgres` and `MapUniqueConstraint`; `/alive` in every environment of the templates) are recorded there too. *Both were closed on 2026-10-08 by ticket #64, as the owner decided: the section below.*

## Wave 2 the PouryaKit prefix on two registrations, and /alive in every environment, ticket #64

Spec #33 and the owner's decisions of 2026-10-08 on the two rows the final structural review left open (13 and 14 of its dispositions, the section above), taken before the pull request to `main`, whose merge publishes the packages for the first time: after it a rename breaks every project generated from the templates, and a template Host without a liveness endpoint cannot run behind an orchestrator. Three items: the rename, `/alive` in every environment of the service defaults recipe, and two wording alignments in `PouryaKit.Cqrs`.

The rename: `UsePostgres<TDbContext>` (`PouryaKit.Persistence.Postgres`) is now `UsePouryaKitPostgres<TDbContext>`, and `MapUniqueConstraint<TDbContext>` (`PouryaKit.Persistence.EFCore`) is now `MapPouryaKitUniqueConstraint<TDbContext>`, with no alias and no forwarder; signatures and behaviour are unchanged. Every section of this record older than this one was proven under the former names, up to `feat/wave-2` at `74b3150` on 2026-10-08. Their API and test names were replaced in place that day, so that each pointer still resolves: the two tests whose names ended `…_naming_use_postgres` and `…_builds_beside_use_postgres` are `CompositionCheckTests.A_module_that_subscribes_but_registers_no_unit_of_work_fails_the_host_when_built_naming_use_pouryakit_postgres` and `MessageStoreDatabaseTests.A_dbcontext_the_application_registered_on_the_message_stores_database_builds_beside_use_pouryakit_postgres`. [ADR 0004](adr/0004-commit-through-wolverine-ef-outbox.md) and [ADR 0006](adr/0006-independent-delivery-through-a-routed-queue-per-subscription.md) keep their text and take a dated amendment each; the plan's section C takes a dated note on its Persistence.EFCore and Persistence.Postgres rows.

Aspire's guidance (https://aka.ms/aspire/healthchecks, "Non-development environments", read on 2026-10-08): its template maps neither health endpoint outside Development, and a project that enables them protects them with host filtering, authorization or both, and considers a request timeout and an output cache against abuse. The recipe maps `/alive` alone there, anonymous by the owner's decision since an orchestrator's probe carries no token, with no request timeout and no output cache because its one check, `self`, is in-process and constant, and leaves host filtering to the deploying project; a Host that tags a check that leaves the process `live`, or maps `/health` outside Development, takes Aspire's protections with it (`host-recipes.md`, "Liveness and readiness").

|---|---|---|

No red run: a rename has no behaviour to switch off, and a caller left behind fails the build. |
| A Host outside Development answers `GET /alive` with 200 `Healthy` to a caller with no credentials, and does not serve `/health` | `HostRecipeTests.Outside_development_liveness_answers_an_anonymous_caller_and_readiness_is_not_served` | A Web Host in Production composed as the templates' `Program.cs` composes one: authentication, `AddPouryaKitWeb`, the service defaults, then `UseRouting`, `UseAuthentication`, `UseAuthorization`, `UseRateLimiter` and `MapDefaultEndpoints`. `/health` answers 401 there: no endpoint serves it, and the authenticated-by-default fallback policy answers the unmatched path; a Host without the Web defaults answers 404. Every earlier `/alive` test runs in Development, so each passes with or without the change. Red once with the Development gate back around `/alive`: 401, a problem response. |
| With the rate limit enabled and the permits of the partition no Actor resolves for used up, `/alive` still answers 200 | `HostRecipeTests.Liveness_answers_while_the_rate_limit_refuses_the_other_callers_no_actor_resolves_for` | The same Host with `PouryaKitRateLimitOptions` enabled at one permit in ten minutes and an anonymous endpoint `/public` outside every Module: `/public` 200, `/alive` 200 twice, `/public` 429. Red once without `DisableRateLimiting()` on the endpoint: `/alive` answered 429 both times, so an anonymous `/alive` call does count in that partition (`ActorRateLimiting`, unchanged), and a probe could be refused by the Host's own limit. |

Wolverine runs `Static` there without a generated registry, which only warns (#58). Red once on both generated solutions, from a pack of the working tree generated as `validate.py` generates them, with the Development gate back in the generated `Extensions.cs`: `/alive` 401 in both. |

Each logged "The Wolverine code generation mode is Static with pre-generated types being loaded from" its own assembly, "Using pre-generated Wolverine HandlerRegistry (0 handler types); skipping assembly scan" and "Hosting environment: Production"; neither publish folder holds `Wolverine.RuntimeCompilation.dll` or `JasperFx.RuntimeCompiler.dll`. The Hosts were stopped and the container removed afterwards. |
| The replaced-container message and the `ICompositionCheck` summary name both callers of `AddPouryaKitCompositionChecks` | `CompositionCheckTests.A_container_that_a_later_configure_container_replaced_stops_the_host_at_start_naming_the_call`, which asserts the message's opening; `OpenApiTests.A_web_host_that_never_installs_the_kits_checks_fails_at_start_naming_the_entry`, the other message, unchanged | Wording only: "which AddPouryaKitModules and UsePouryaKitWolverine call". The summary no longer says a Host's checks run at two moments: its two contracts have a moment each, and a Host that runs Wolverine is checked at a third, as `modules.md` and `messaging.md` count them. `AddPouryaKitCompositionChecks`'s summary says "the composition checks and the startup checks" where it said "both moments' checks". |

A project that reorders its own pipeline is told by `host-recipes.md`. |

## Wave 2 the findings of the pull request's AI reviews that hold, ticket #66

Spec #33. Two AI reviewers left 42 threads on pull request #65, which merges Wave 2 into `main` and so publishes the packages for the first time, at `58adc1d`. Every claim was checked against the code on 2026-10-08, and this ticket builds what held, with the owner's decisions of that day on the findings that add a guarantee or change a recorded key; the other threads are answered in the pull request. Two behaviours stay as they were by the owner's decision of the same day: a command the application owns in a Host of Modules still fails at its first dispatch, naming the fix, not at the build (thread T29), and the bearer scheme's `ClockSkew` takes no cap (T03).

Both keys now name a type as `Namespace.Type, Assembly`, its full name and its assembly's simple name ([ADR 0002](adr/0002-no-mediator-dependency.md)'s #66 amendment). One member became public for the refusal of `Schedule`: `Operation.IsUnitOfWorkActive` of `PouryaKit.Cqrs`, by the main session's decision of 2026-10-08. `PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine` has to know whether a Unit of Work runs, `Operation.MayChangeState` is true in a scope that runs none, and [ADR 0007](adr/0007-operation-context-set-by-the-scope-opener.md) with `TrustedRestoreTests` keeps Cqrs's internals closed to that package but for the trusted restore, so the read-only signal joined `MayChangeState`, `Module` and `Context` on the public `Operation`.

|---|---|---|

| Two `[Idempotent]` commands of one full name in two assemblies, sent by the same Actor and tenant with the same request id, each run, and neither replays the other | `SameNamedTypeTests.Two_idempotent_commands_of_one_full_name_in_two_assemblies_each_run_under_one_request_id`, the same application, on the key table of the `stub` schema in PostgreSQL, which then holds two rows of that request id; `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests.IdempotencyTests` pins the key's form at the dispatcher | With the assembly left out of the idempotency key, the second command replayed `ModularMonolith.Tests`, the first type's recorded success. |
| `IDelayedCommands.Schedule` called in a scope whose Operation runs no Unit of Work throws, saying the command would never be stored and who may schedule, and stores nothing: in a Host of Modules, a begun scope that sends no command and a scope never begun; in an application without Modules, a begun scope | `DelayedCommandTests.Scheduling_in_a_scope_of_a_host_of_modules_that_runs_no_unit_of_work_throws_and_stores_nothing` (both scopes) and `Scheduling_in_a_scope_of_an_application_without_modules_that_runs_no_unit_of_work_throws`, which assert the message in full | With the guard off, the call returned in the begun scope of each Host, and `wolverine_incoming_envelopes` stayed empty once the fixture Host's scope was gone: the command was dropped. ## Wave 2 a repository write outside a Unit of Work is refused, ticket #67

Spec #33. The owner's instructions of 2026-10-08, after ticket #66: find the repository writes made outside a command, refuse the silent loss with a clear exception without breaking a legitimate use, and record in an ADR the decision that made `Operation.IsUnitOfWorkActive` public. With them, the two findings of Codex's second review of pull request #65 that hold (T43, T44).

The repository's two write members, `AddAsync` and `Remove`, checked only `Operation.MayChangeState`, which is true in a scope that runs no command, so the DbContext tracked the change and the scope dropped it. They now also refuse where `Operation.IsUnitOfWorkActive` is false, after the reading check, with `The repository of {Aggregate} was asked to write while no Unit of Work is active, so no commit would save the change and it would be lost. Write from a command's handler, a Domain Event Handler, or a Notification or Integration Event Handler.` No read member and no constructor is guarded, no public member was added, and `IUnitOfWorkConnection.Join()` keeps its own check. Every call of `AddAsync` and `Remove` in `src/`, `samples/`, `tests/`, the templates and the clean consumers was read before the guard was written: each is in a command's handler, a Domain Event Handler, a Notification Handler or an Integration Event Handler, a delayed command's run, or one of the two cases the reading check already refuses.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| In a Host of Modules, `AddAsync` and `Remove` called in a begun scope that sends no command, and in one whose command has already committed, throw the message in full, naming the aggregate and who may write, and `stub.notes` holds what it held: the note to add is absent and the note to remove is still there | `RepositoryTests.A_repository_write_in_a_scope_of_a_host_of_modules_that_runs_no_unit_of_work_throws_and_saves_nothing`, both scopes, against PostgreSQL: the note to remove is found through the repository in the same scope, which needs no Unit of Work | With the check off, in both scopes: the stored rows were as asserted, so nothing was saved, and `AddAsync` had returned (`Assert.IsType() Failure: Value is null`). With the guard left out of `Remove` alone, `Remove` returned the same way and the row stayed. |
| In an application without Modules, the same in a begun scope and in a scope never begun, where no Module's router refuses first | `SingleModuleTests.A_repository_write_in_a_scope_that_runs_no_unit_of_work_throws_and_saves_nothing`, both scopes, on a plain service collection over the Stub's DbContext | With the check off, in both scopes: the stored rows were as asserted and `AddAsync` had returned; with the guard left out of `Remove`, so had `Remove`. |
| In a Host of Modules, a scope whose Operation never began cannot resolve the repository at all: `The Unit of Work was needed in a scope whose Operation names no Module. …`, the router's message | `RepositoryTests.A_repository_is_refused_in_a_scope_of_a_host_of_modules_whose_operation_never_began` | No red run: it pins what the router of `AddPouryaKitModules` did before this ticket, which the ticket leaves as it is. |
| Code that only reads still gets its own message: a query sent on its own, which runs no Unit of Work either, is told it only reads | `RepositoryTests.A_query_sent_on_its_own_that_adds_through_the_repository_is_told_it_only_reads`; beside the two earlier tests of a query nested in a command and of a Snapshot mapper, `RepositoryTests.A_query_sent_from_a_command_cannot_add_an_aggregate_through_the_repository` and `SnapshotTests.A_snapshot_mapper_that_sends_a_command_or_writes_is_refused_and_nothing_commits`, both unchanged | With the Unit of Work check ahead of the reading check, the query was told `The repository of Note was asked to write while no Unit of Work is active …`. The two earlier tests cannot go red that way: a Unit of Work is active in both. |
| Every read member works with no Unit of Work, and every caller that writes inside one keeps writing | Unchanged and green: `SingleModuleTests.A_query_sent_through_the_dispatcher_reads_without_the_modularity_package` and `PersistenceTests.The_generic_repository_finds_by_id_and_finds_lists_checks_and_counts_by_specification` (each read member, in a query sent on its own), `PagingTests` (a repository built in a begun scope that runs no command), and the classes that write through a repository: `RepositoryTests`, `UnitOfWorkTests`, `DomainEventTests`, `PersistencePreparationTests`, `ConflictTests`, `PersistenceTests`, `ReadOnlyStateTests`, `SnapshotTests`, with the delivery, delayed command and sample tests of the whole suite | No red run: no existing test was changed, and none failed under the refusal. |
| ADR and docs | The amendment of 2026-10-08 at the end of [ADR 0003](adr/0003-single-commit-owner.md): the public `Operation.IsUnitOfWorkActive` and why, the rule and the three places the Kit applies it, what the rule cannot cover, and the three alternatives rejected. The summaries of `IRepository.AddAsync` and `Remove` and of `EfRepository`; `persistence.md` and `commands-queries-validation.md` of the Kit's skill; `docs/architecture/README.md` (Operation state, the repository's DbContext) | |
| T43: the summary of `AddPouryaKitPermissions` said a continuation "whose context has a causation" is not checked again; it is a trusted continuation, and a causation alone does not make one | The summaries of `AddPouryaKitPermissions` and `IActorPermissions`, and the permission check's line in `docs/provenance/PouryaKit.Security.md`. `PermissionAuthorizer` and its tests are unchanged: `SecurityTests.A_context_application_code_builds_with_a_causation_is_not_trusted_and_its_actor_is_checked` already proves the behaviour | Doc only. |
| T44: composition problems are reported when a singleton's `Dispose` throws; one guarded disposal serves this path and #66's, where a check throws | `CompositionCheckTests.Composition_problems_fail_the_build_with_every_problem_though_a_singletons_disposal_throws`: `Build` throws `InvalidOperationException` with every problem, and the other resolved singleton is disposed | With the disposal unguarded on the problems path, as it was: `Build` threw the disposal's `NotSupportedException` in place of the problems. |
| The failed build's container is disposed as the Host disposes its own, asynchronously, so a singleton that is only `IAsyncDisposable` is disposed too (added on 2026-10-08 after the ticket closed, for the limit its closing comment recorded) | `CompositionCheckTests.A_failed_build_disposes_a_singleton_that_can_only_be_disposed_asynchronously` | `Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting`'s own `Host.Dispose()` awaits `DisposeAsync()` the same way (10.0.12). Red once with the synchronous `Dispose`, as it was: the container's `Dispose` threw for that singleton, which stayed undisposed with what was created before it. |
| Out of scope | | A change to `Operation.MayChangeState`, to `Join`'s check, to a read member or to the repository's constructor; a guard on the Kit's own write seams. |

The rule cannot cover a change made directly to an aggregate loaded through the repository, or a Domain Event raised on one, in a scope that runs no command: no call of the Kit is made there, so nothing can refuse it, and the tracked-state comparison runs only at the end of a query, a check or a reading step. The Kit's own write seams (`IReceipts`, the Outbox buffers, `IUnitOfWork.DbContext`) stay unguarded because the structure test closes them to a Module; a Host's own assembly is not judged by it. Both are recorded in the ADR's amendment.

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch` on the ticket's source (commit `76ab4b8`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.142`, the version its manifest records for that commit.

## Wave 2 final audit before the pull request to main

The owner's instruction of 2026-10-08, before pull request #65 merges: no unfinished Wave 2 requirement, known actionable defect or unresolved review finding. Five read-only scouts read every Wave 2 ticket (#34 to #67) against its acceptance criteria, closing comment, code and tests, the nine ADRs with their amendments, every recorded limit, and the `origin/main...HEAD` diff for debt. No acceptance criterion was found unmet and no review finding open. What they found, and what was done:

| Found | Done | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| A failed build's container was disposed synchronously, so a singleton that is only `IAsyncDisposable` stayed undisposed with what was created before it (#67's recorded limit) | `ValidatingServiceProviderFactory` awaits `DisposeAsync()`, as `Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting`'s `Host.Dispose()` does; the guard that keeps the build's own failure stays | `CompositionCheckTests.A_failed_build_disposes_a_singleton_that_can_only_be_disposed_asynchronously`, red once with the synchronous disposal (the #67 section above). The thread has no synchronization context while it waits (`SynchronizationContextScope`), so a disposal that awaits does not wait for the thread it blocks: `CompositionCheckTests.A_failed_build_on_a_thread_with_a_synchronization_context_still_ends_and_disposes_what_awaits`, red once without the scope, where the build never ended (the pull request's review, 2026-10-09) |
| The service defaults recipes are adapted from .NET Aspire's ServiceDefaults template and ship in `PouryaKit.Templates` with no provenance record and no notice (#52, #53) | A record in [ModularMonolith's provenance](provenance/ModularMonolith.md#the-service-defaults-recipes) with the upstream commit and path, and upstream's MIT licence text in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`, which every package carries | The package validation below compares each packed notices file with the root one |
| The repository's refusal named who may write and left out the Integration Event Handler, which writes through a repository in the Proving Sample (#67) | The message ends "a Domain Event Handler, or a Notification or Integration Event Handler" | `RepositoryTests` and `SingleModuleTests` assert the message in full |
| The message store's database line credited `UsePouryaKitPostgres` for every named DbContext, though `AddPouryaKitEfCore<TDbContext>(connectionName)` names one too (#63) | The line says "{DbContext} is stored over the connection string {name}", as `DbContextConnectionsCheck` has named none since the final review | `MessageStoreDatabaseTests` asserts the five messages in full |
| `INotificationOutbox`'s summary listed Notifications and Integration Events only, while the Outbox check also counts Snapshots and delayed or recurring commands (#44) | The summary and the architecture document list all of them | Doc only |
| Fifteen `using` directives Wave 2 added or orphaned, which the build does not report | Removed | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`, 0 warnings |
| Wording that later tickets made untrue: five rows above named tests the final review renamed from `Startup_fails_` to `Building_fails_`, and three of them spoke of the Host's start; the architecture document's "two moments"; the sample's `CallerPermissions` comment said "a continuation"; ADR 0002's #61 amendment named one caller of the checks entry and no startup check | Corrected in place; ADR 0002 takes a dated note | `check-docs.py`; each `Class.Method` in this file resolves |
| A repository taken outside the dispatcher's pipeline: can a structure rule refuse it? | No rule over Modules was added. A Module of a Host of Modules cannot reach the shape, since an endpoint's scope names no Module and resolving a repository there throws; the structure tests of Modules judge only such Modules, where the rule would refuse nothing, and a service that a handler calls is a legitimate consumer it would refuse. An application without Modules can reach it: since ticket #68 a rule judges the monolith template's own files, and code an application adds after `dotnet new` stays unjudged, as ADR 0003 and `persistence.md` say | [#68](https://github.com/PouryaNoufallah96/PouryaKit/issues/68), [its section below](#wave-2-a-repository-taken-outside-the-dispatchers-pipeline-ticket-68) |

Kept by the owner's decision: a Host's own assembly keeps its access to the Kit's write seams.

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch` on the audit's source (commit `d12137a`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.142`, as its manifest gives it: the generated solutions compile the service defaults without the removed `using`, and every packed notices file equals the root one, with the .NET Aspire section. On that commit `ModularMonolith.Tests` ran 561 passed, `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 99 and `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests` 44.

## Wave 2 a repository taken outside the dispatcher's pipeline, ticket #68

The follow-up the final audit opened ([its row above](#wave-2-final-audit-before-the-pull-request-to-main)). The owner's decision of 2026-10-09: a text rule over the monolith template's own files, the wording of the gap corrected after a runtime proof, and the ticket closed with its limit named. Tests and docs only: no package, no public member and no runtime behaviour changed.

In an application without Modules the request scope holds the application's own Unit of Work, so an endpoint or a service that takes a repository can load an aggregate and change it outside a command, and no call of the Kit is made there to refuse it. ADR 0003 and the Kit's skill said such a change "is still lost without a word". Run against PostgreSQL, that holds where the scope sends no command; a command the scope sends after the change, if it succeeds, saves it.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| None of the monolith template's own files, its Composition Root, its five endpoints, the Host's `Program.cs` and the smoke test both templates share, takes a repository: no parameter, field or local of `IRepository<,>` or of an interface the Proving Sample derives from it (`IProductRepository` today, read from the sample's files), and none asked of a service provider. The Composition Root's registration of `IProductRepository` takes nothing and is not refused | `TemplateTests.None_of_the_monoliths_own_files_takes_a_repository`: the rule `TemplateRules.RepositoriesTaken` over `content/monolith` and `content/shared`, read as text. Its offending set gets exactly three messages: an endpoint whose lambda takes `IRepository<Product, ProductId>`, a service whose constructor takes `IProductRepository`, and a `Program.cs` that asks a service provider for one under a comment that names `IProductRepository`. Each names the file and the fix: `… takes IProductRepository; send a command through IDispatcher instead: a handler takes the repository, and a change made outside a command is dropped or saved by a command that never made it.` | Six runs, each with one part of the rule off, and the test failed in each: the rule reading no file (no message); the derived names not read from the sample (the service's message missing); comments not left out (a fourth message, for the comment); a declaration not counted (the endpoint's and the service's messages missing); a service provider's answer not counted (the `Program.cs` message missing); and any naming counted, where the shipped `CatalogModule.cs` was refused for its registration. |
| In an application without Modules, an aggregate found through the repository in a begun scope that sends no command, and changed there, is dropped with the scope: nothing throws and its row keeps what it held | `SingleModuleTests.A_change_to_a_loaded_aggregate_in_a_scope_that_sends_no_command_is_dropped_without_a_word`, on a plain service collection over the Stub's DbContext: a folder created by a command, then found and relabelled in a second scope | No red run: the test shows a limit, not a guarantee, so there is no behaviour to switch off. Observed: the scope ended without an exception and `stub.folders` kept `grey`. |
| The same, then a query sent through `IDispatcher` in that scope: the query is not refused and the change is still dropped, because the tracked-state comparison's baseline is the query's start, which already holds the change | `SingleModuleTests.A_change_to_a_loaded_aggregate_is_not_refused_by_a_query_sent_after_it_and_is_still_dropped` | No red run, for the same reason. Observed: the query succeeded and `stub.folders` kept `grey`. A probe in the same composition, not kept, had the query itself relabel the folder after the change made outside it, and it threw `A query changed a Label the Operation saves. Code that only reads must leave the tracked state unchanged.`: the comparison runs there, and its silence in the test is the baseline's. |
| The same, then a command sent through `IDispatcher` in that scope, which writes another row and succeeds: its commit saves the change made outside it, since the Unit of Work saves the whole DbContext | `SingleModuleTests.A_change_to_a_loaded_aggregate_is_saved_by_the_commit_of_a_command_sent_after_it`: `WriteNoteCommand` writes a note, and the folder's label is stored beside it | No red run, for the same reason. Observed: `stub.folders` held `blue` and `stub.notes` the command's note. |
| ADR and docs | The note of 2026-10-09 under "What the rule cannot cover" in [ADR 0003](adr/0003-single-commit-owner.md)'s amendment of 2026-10-08; `persistence.md`, `commands-queries-validation.md` and `templates.md` of the Kit's skill; `docs/architecture/README.md` (the repository's DbContext, the `TemplateTests` row); the audit's row above | |
| Out of scope | | An exception message or a behaviour. The refused writes of #66 and #67 keep their messages, and the summaries under `src/` that repeat their reason, "no commit would save the change", stay as written: that is exact for a scope that sends no command or whose command has returned, and a write made before the scope's command is refused either way, so no test can show what it would do. One `//` comment in `EfRepository.cs` said "no commit would ever save it" without that limit; the review found it (R2) and, on the owner's word of 2026-10-09, it now says the change would be dropped with the scope or saved by a command the scope sent after it. A comment only: no member, message or behaviour changed. The modular template: a Module of a Host of Modules cannot reach the shape. The three tests change a value; none raises a Domain Event. |

What stays open: code an application adds after `dotnet new` is not judged. The rule reads the files the Kit ships, and no package ships structure rules, so a generated solution carries none. As text, the rule also cannot see a repository reached through an alias, through `typeof`, inside another generic type, or through another type the file takes that itself takes one; its summary says so.
