It ran in full: the changed tests are paths the validation builds and runs, so `--since` did not skip. On that commit `ModularMonolith.Tests` ran 565 passed and `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 100.

## Pre-Wave 3 WolverineFx 6.48.2, ticket #75

Ticket #75 of spec #74 (finding P2), first and alone so that every later ticket of the initiative builds on it: the five `WolverineFx*` pins move from 6.46.0 to 6.48.2. In 6.46.0, one `ReconnectedAsync` exception while Wolverine rebuilt its RabbitMQ agents after a connection recovery left that listener and every one after it without a consumer, while health stayed green; 6.48.0 repairs it (`JasperFx/wolverine` #4864, #4867). [ADR 0004](adr/0004-commit-through-wolverine-ef-outbox.md)'s #75 amendment records the move. No Kit package's code changed.

The release notes, read on 2026-10-09:

- 6.48.0: one listener failing to rebuild after a connection recovery no longer strands the listeners behind it; each is rebuilt on its own, a failure is retried on a bounded schedule, and channel teardown tolerates a channel the client already closed (#4864, #4867). Its other fixes are for clusters (a route resolves its sending agent on every use; global partition slots; agent restrictions confirmed) and `[Deduplicated]` windows.
- 6.48.1: a global partition slot's backlog resumes on its new owner in the order it was sent (#4863).
- 6.48.2: the leader's matching of agents to nodes, and the rest of its first evaluation, are no longer quadratic in the agent count (#4886, #4893); a newly elected leader holds first-time placements for `DurabilitySettings.LeaderTakeoverHoldEvaluations` evaluations, default 1, standing down while no other node holds an assignment (#4899); lifecycle and soak coverage.
- Neither 6.48.1 nor 6.48.2 changes a path the Kit takes, so 6.48.2 was taken, as the ticket names it. 6.48.3, published at 14:42 UTC the same day after the ticket was cut, spreads blue/green warm-ups over every joining node (#4904, #4909) and commits a chain that reads through `IQuerySession` and sends (#4910, a Marten, Polecat and Fisher seam); it was not taken.

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| The five `WolverineFx*` pins are 6.48.2 and no other pin changes; the templates' `WolverineFx.RuntimeCompilation` pin follows | `Directory.Packages.props`; both templates' `Directory.Packages.props`, which `TemplateTests` keeps equal to the Kit's | On nuget.org each package is `MIT` with a `net10.0` asset. Against 6.46.0 their only dependency change is `JasperFx`, `JasperFx.Events` and `JasperFx.SourceGenerator` 2.80.2 to 2.81.0, `MIT`. Across the checkout's projects restore moved only those three and `WolverineFx.RDBMS` and `WolverineFx.Newtonsoft`, to 6.48.2; it resolved 312 package versions before and after, the same packages by name, Weasel still 9.41.0 |
| The Kit builds and every test passes on 6.48.2 | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors. `dotnet test PouryaKit.slnx`: 829 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped | `ModularMonolith.Tests` ran in 2m25s and 2m27s in two full runs, against 2m23s at `505fd44`, where the suite had 828. No test failed or slowed, so none was run again on its own. The started test Hosts run Solo (`UsePouryaKitTestDelivery`); the Balanced ones are single nodes, such as `Inventory.Host` as a process in Production, where `LeaderTakeoverHoldEvaluations` stands down |
| A Host whose broker connections drop while it runs consumes its queue again, and a publication committed after the drop arrives once | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.A_host_whose_broker_connections_drop_while_it_runs_consumes_its_queue_again_and_a_publication_committed_after_arrives` | `BrokerFixture.DropConnectionsAsync` runs `rabbitmqctl close_all_connections -p <vhost>` inside the broker's container while both Hosts run. The test waits for the Inventory Host's queue to show no consumer, which proves the drop reached its listener, then for a consumer again, then commits a product in Catalog and reads its stock item and Receipt. Passed on 6.48.2 (6.8 s) and on 6.46.0 (6.7 s, the five pins set back in the worktree, the test alone). It is therefore not a regression test for #4867: a single broker's forced close makes no rebuild throw, and the defect needs one that does, which upstream's own test injects through a fake agent. No red run: none could be built without engineering that exception. No other test drops a connection while its Hosts live; the recovery tests kill and restart Hosts |
| Docs | ADR 0004's #75 amendment; plan I.3, the RabbitMQ transport row; [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md) (the RabbitMQ transport's package); the provenance of `PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine`, `.EFCore`, `.RabbitMq`, `PouryaKit.Testing` and the Proving Sample; the Kit skill's `host-recipes.md`, whose statement that Wolverine registers no RabbitMQ health check still holds on 6.48.2 (a `RabbitMqHealthCheck` type ships, and nothing in `Wolverine` or `Wolverine.RabbitMQ` registers it) | |
| Out of scope | | Lines that record behaviour observed or source read on 6.46.0 stay as dated facts: earlier sections of this file, the skill's `testing.md`, the `V6.46.0` citations of ADRs 0004, 0006 and 0009, the package table of ADR 0006's #50 amendment, and the comments in `Subscriptions.cs`. Plan I.3's `WolverineFx.Marten` 6.46.0 names a planned Capability, not a pin |

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since 505fd44` on the ticket's source (commit `f7abadc`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.23`, the version its manifest records for that commit. It ran in full: the pins reach every package, so `--since` did not skip, and the packed packages, their clean consumers and both generated solutions restored and built on WolverineFx 6.48.2.

**Follow-up, 2026-10-09: WolverineFx 6.48.3.** At the owner's request, 6.48.3, which the list above records as not taken, was reviewed and run, then taken: the seven pins moved from 6.48.2 to 6.48.3 and nothing else. Its notes: a blue/green warm-up spreads over every green node that joins (#4904); three gaps in that machinery, found by a soak on real dispatcher lanes, are closed (#4909); fleet-scale simulated scenarios, test code only (#4905); a chain that only reads through `IQuerySession` but sends gets its commit, in the Marten, Polecat and Fisher frame providers (#4910, #4912). The tag comparison (16 commits, 21 files) also holds #4903, which the notes leave out: `IgnoreEnvelopes` applies inside a tracked test session's `AddStage` stages. The Kit uses no Marten, Polecat, Fisher, event subscription or tracked session. #4904 and #4909 change the leader's agent placement (`AgentCommandDispatcher`, `AssignmentGrid`, `NodeAgentController.EvaluateAssignments`), which every Balanced node runs, but their new branches act only when the leader moves an agent between nodes, which a single node never does. The started test Hosts run Solo; `Inventory.Host` as its own process in Production is a single Balanced node, and `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.A_product_catalog_creates_is_applied_by_inventory_host_running_as_its_own_process_with_the_operation_context` passed on 6.48.3. No test runs several Balanced nodes on one store, on this release or the last. The nuspecs on nuget.org and in the NuGet cache after the restore agree: each package is `MIT` with a `net10.0` asset, and only dependencies among the `WolverineFx` packages move; restore resolved 312 package versions before and after, which differ only in the seven `WolverineFx*` packages, `WolverineFx.RDBMS` and `WolverineFx.Newtonsoft` among them, while `JasperFx` 2.81.0, Weasel 9.41.0 and `RabbitMQ.Client` 7.2.2 stay. No file of `Wolverine.RabbitMQ` and no health check changed between the tags, so `host-recipes.md`'s statement that Wolverine registers no RabbitMQ health check holds on 6.48.3. The listener repair is the one 6.48.2 took: #4867 (merge commit `cbb1c4e`, 2026-10-07) is an ancestor of V6.48.0, V6.48.2 and V6.48.3 and not of V6.46.0, and the decompiled `Wolverine.RabbitMQ.dll` of 6.46.0 and 6.48.2 differ in exactly its two files, `ConnectionMonitor.cs` (each agent rebuilt in its own try/catch, a failed rebuild retried after 1, 2, 5, 10, 20 and 30 s) and `RabbitMqChannelAgent.cs` (a teardown that tolerates a closed channel). The drop test above proves recovery after a clean drop and delivery after it, not #4867, and no regression test that uses only the broker exists: every refusal a single-node broker can produce on demand closes the channel at channel level, and 6.46.0's unchanged channel-shutdown hook then rebuilds again, while the exceptions that strand a listener (the client's own disposal errors, a quorum queue without a majority) cannot be produced on demand, and upstream's own regression test injects an internal fake agent the Kit cannot build. The defect was reproduced on neither version. A Kit Host has a second broker listener, not durable in Wolverine's sense whatever the broker lists (its endpoint mode is Inline; decompiled `RabbitMqQueue.cs:258`, `RabbitMqTransport.cs:557-560`): by Wolverine's default `DeclareRequestReplySystemQueue = true` (decompiled `RabbitMqTransport.cs:145`), it listens on an auto-delete `wolverine.response.<guid>` queue through the same listening connection (decompiled `RabbitMqListener.cs:88`), so "every one after it" above can apply to a Kit Host. One such queue per Host, with one consumer, was listed by `rabbitmqctl list_queues -p <vhost>` in the broker's container while the drop test ran; the shared connection is read from the code. A known limit, not a Kit defect: after its last retry, about 68 s after the recovery, 6.48.x gives up, and the agent stays without a consumer until the next connection recovery, still with no health signal. On 6.48.3, `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx` has 0 warnings and 0 errors; `RabbitMqDeliveryTests` passed 21 (the drop test in 6.7 s), `RecurringCommandTests` 9 and `DelayedCommandTests` 13; `dotnet test PouryaKit.slnx` passed 830, 0 failed, 0 skipped, `ModularMonolith.Tests` in 2m29s against about 2m27s at `687acf6`, where the suite also had 830. `python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since 687acf6` on the follow-up's source (commit `900a80c`, before this sentence was added) printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.30`, the version its manifest records for that commit; it ran in full, since the pins reach every package.

## Pre-Wave 3 check-docs path citations and qualified pointers, ticket #76

Ticket #76 of spec #74 (finding D14, "Prerequisites before any file moves"): `check-docs.py` gains the two rules that prove the file moves of #78 and #79 break no record. Before it, the script verified no cited path and no acceptance pointer qualified by its namespace, so a moved file would have left every citation of it stale with every check green. The check script, its unit tests and the docs changed; no package, no public member and no runtime behaviour.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| A Kit path the docs cite names a file or folder that git tracks or would track and the working tree holds: each path on the **Kit files** line of a provenance record, and each span that is a path into `src/PouryaKit.*`, `samples/` or `tests/` in the rest of a record, but its **Upstream paths** line, or in a top-level `docs/*.md`, the plan and this record among them. A `:line` suffix is not part of the path, and a path holding `*` is a glob that must match one | `CheckDocsTests.test_a_cited_kit_file_that_does_not_exist_fails`: a missing file on a record's Kit files line, one in the record's prose and one cited with a line in the plan, each with its own `FAIL` line. `CheckDocsTests.test_cited_kit_paths_that_exist_pass`: a Kit files path, and in the plan a path with a line range, a folder and a glob | The first failed against the script before this ticket: nothing was reported. It failed again against the script of the first review (`09d2ecb`), which read a record's Kit files line only, and with the records' prose switched off: the prose citation was not reported. In copies of the script with one part switched off: a `:line` suffix read as part of the path, and the first failed (the plan's citation was no longer read as a path); a glob read as a literal path, and the second failed |
| Out of the path rule by design: an upstream repository's path, which a record cites under **Upstream paths** or as `repository@commit path` and the plan as a path outside the Kit's packages; the test project of a solution generated from the templates (`tests/acme-shop.Tests`), which exists only during a `validate.py` run; every path in `docs/adr/`, dated records | `CheckDocsTests.test_an_upstream_path_in_a_provenance_record_or_the_plan_passes`, `CheckDocsTests.test_a_generated_consumer_project_in_the_acceptance_record_passes`, `CheckDocsTests.test_a_path_an_adr_cites_passes` | Each exception switched off in a copy of the script, and its test failed: every line of a record read, not only Kit files (the upstream test); a record's Upstream paths line read like its prose (the upstream test, on its `tests/` path); all of `src/` read as the Kit's packages (the upstream and the generated tests); the generated names not recognised (the generated test); `docs/adr/` read (the ADR test) |
| A pointer in this record qualified by its namespace, `Namespace.Class.Method` or `Namespace.Class`, names a class of that name in a test file whose first namespace is that one, and the method when it names one. A span that is a test file's namespace, such as `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests`, names a project and is no pointer | `CheckDocsTests.test_a_qualified_pointer_naming_no_declared_test_fails`: a method the class lacks, the class under another namespace and an undeclared class, each with its own `FAIL` line. `CheckDocsTests.test_acceptance_pointers_to_existing_tests_pass`, whose fixture gained a namespace, a pointer of each qualified form and a project's name | The first failed against the script before this ticket: nothing was reported. With the namespace dropped from the lookup, it failed on the class under another namespace; with a project's name read as a pointer, the second failed |
| Today's tree | `python3 .agents/scripts/check-docs.py`: 0 failures in 94 Markdown files | Counted on the ticket's tree before this section was added. The path rule checked 136 cited paths: 94 on the Kit files lines of 18 provenance records (86 distinct), 18 in the prose of 5 records (13 distinct), and 24 in top-level docs (this record 14, the plan 8, `docs/packages.md` and `docs/private-packages.md` one each, two of them globs); it left out `tests/acme-shop.Tests` once. Among the records' paths are the 31 the spec counts in the four packages that #78 reorganizes, 25 on Kit files lines and 6 in prose. The pointer rule checked 15 qualified pointers, 11 distinct: 11 name a method and 4 a class; it read `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 7 times as a project. The ticket counted 13 from a grep; the rule reads 15. The 401 bare pointers are read as before |
| Drift the rules exposed | Two citations, fixed in the plan and in a provenance record | The plan's section I, on the endpoint adapter, cited `CreateProductEndpoint.cs` with its folders elided, which names no path. It now cites the file's full path, at line 23, where the endpoint takes `IGatewayProcessor<CatalogModule>`; line 18 held that parameter when the citation was written (`f074dfb`). The record of `PouryaKit.Testing`, under its intentional differences, cited an upstream test fixture as a path of its own after naming the repository and the commit apart; it now cites it as `repository@commit path`, as the other records do. No pointer had drifted |
| Docs | The docstring of `check-docs.py` names both rules; `docs/agents/ai-setup.md`'s list of what `check-docs.py` fails on names them too. Its "Check scripts" row names no rule and is unchanged | |
| Out of scope | | A `:line` suffix's number is not checked, and a path inside a command line (`dotnet test --project …`) is no citation. `python3 -m unittest .agents/scripts/test_check_docs.py` stops with `ValueError: Empty module name`, because a module name cannot start with the dot of `.agents`; the `ai-setup` job's command, `python3 -m unittest discover -s .agents/scripts -p 'test_*.py'`, ran the 60 unit tests beside the check scripts, all passed |

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since 1a5803e` on the ticket's source after its review's fix round (commit `ac0481c`, before this paragraph was last changed) printed `SKIP: 9 path(s) differ from 1a5803e, and none is one a package, a template or this validation is built from. CI runs the full validation on every push and pull request.`: the check script, its tests, the docs and the agent memory reach no package. The run on the first source, `282487c`, had printed the same with 8 paths.

## Pre-Wave 3 the namespace policy, ticket #77

Ticket #77 of spec #74 (decision D14): the namespace policy, stated once in [ADR 0010](adr/0010-one-namespace-per-kit-package.md) and enforced once on each side, on today's flat tree before any file moves (ticket #78). A Kit package has folders by responsibility and one namespace, the package name; application code's namespaces follow its folders. No namespace changed, and no file under `src/` changed.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| Every authored top-level type of each Kit package is in the one namespace named after its assembly. Compiler-generated definitions are left out by their marker, `CompilerGeneratedAttribute` or `GeneratedCodeAttribute`: today `<PrivateImplementationDetails>`, anonymous types and collection-expression types in the global namespace, the `GeneratedRegex` output in `System.Text.RegularExpressions.Generated` (Messaging) and the OpenAPI XML comment generator's output in `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OpenApi.Generated` and `System.Runtime.CompilerServices` (Web.OpenApi) | `NamespaceTests.Every_type_of_a_kit_package_is_in_the_namespace_named_after_it`, over the 20 packages of code under `src/` (`Sample.KitPackages`) | With the generated-code exclusion off, it failed on 35 definitions in 11 packages, those listed. With the nested-type filter off, it failed on the nested types of generated ones, such as `` <>z__ReadOnlySingleElementList`1+Enumerator `` and the regex's `RunnerFactory`, which carry no marker of their own |
| An authored type whose namespace is not its assembly's name is rejected, in a sub-namespace (the editor's folder default) and in an unrelated namespace | `NamespaceTests.A_type_outside_its_packages_namespace_is_rejected`: in the assembly `Offending`, whose other types are in `Offending`, the rule names exactly `Offending.Stray.StrayType`, declared in `tests/PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests/Offending/Stray/StrayType.cs` with its folder's namespace; `Offending.Core` compiles Core's sources and `TextHelpers`, all in `PouryaKit.Core`, into the assembly `Offending.Core`, and the rule names exactly those four types. Each assembly's generated definitions are not among them | The positive test's check, run over `Sample.KitPackages` with `Offending.Core` added, failed with those four messages. In the fix round of review R1: with the equality weakened to a prefix check, the test failed on `Offending`, which then had no violation; with only sub-namespaces rejected, it failed on `Offending.Core`, which then had none |
| IDE0130 is a warning in the Proving Sample's Modules and the test projects, so a namespace there that does not follow its folder fails the build; the three exceptions of ADR 0010 are `none` | `.editorconfig`; `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors | Before the move below, the build failed with one error: `Lookup.cs(1,11): error IDE0130: Namespace "PouryaKit.Core.Tests.Lookup" does not match folder structure, expected "PouryaKit.Core.Tests"`. With the `none` of `StubSameNamed.cs` off, its project failed (`expected "ModularMonolith.Tests.SameNamed"`); with that of `TextHelpers.cs` off, `Offending.Core` failed (`expected "Offending.Core"`). The service defaults' `none` cannot go red: the warning's scope does not reach that file |
| Core.Tests' `Lookup.cs` sits in `Lookup/` with its namespace, `PouryaKit.Core.Tests.Lookup`, unchanged | `git diff -M --stat 1a5803e -- tests/PouryaKit.Core.Tests`: `Lookup.cs => Lookup/Lookup.cs`, a 100% rename with no line changed; `PouryaKit.Core.Tests`: 17 passed | |
| Nothing else changed behaviour | `dotnet test PouryaKit.slnx`: 831 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (#75 recorded 829; this ticket adds the two above) | |
| Docs and AI guidance | ADR 0010; the "Namespaces" line of `.claude/rules/kit-code.md`; [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md), the `NamespaceTests` row and the offending fixtures' paragraph | |
| Out of scope | | Any namespace, and any file under `src/`: ticket #78 moves the files. The templates' generated solutions, which ADR 0010 leaves out of the enforcement |

The warning's scope is the spec's. Measured on 2026-10-09 with IDE0130 a warning for every C# file: with the three `none` exceptions and the move, the build still had 0 warnings, so both Hosts and the service defaults follow their folders today but for the excepted `Extensions.cs`.

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since 1a5803e` on the ticket's source (commit `7086ce8`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.23`, the version its manifest records for that commit. It ran in full: `.editorconfig` and the test projects are paths the validation builds from, so `--since` did not skip.

A test's body changed only where the table says: the four tests that moved, verbatim, to the classes of their behaviours, and the one expected message that names a renamed type.

The cuts left usings that the build refuses as unused (IDE0005): five go from `BrokerFixture.cs` and one from `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.cs`. ## Pre-Wave 3 sample handler names, Module settings and Npgsql tracing, ticket #80

Ticket #80 of spec #74 (findings D14's sample rename, V7 and V16): three changes to the Proving Sample, its service defaults and the Kit's skill. All of them reach both templates, which pack the sample's Modules and `ModularMonolith.ServiceDefaults/Extensions.cs` when `PouryaKit.Templates` is packed. No Kit package's code changed and no package is added.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| Database commands appear in an Operation's trace: the service defaults' tracing adds Npgsql's own source, `Npgsql`, beside the Kit's, and in `Inventory.Host` the delivery Operation of `inventory.product-created` has `Npgsql` spans whose parent is its `pouryakit.operation` span | `HostRecipeTests.Inventory_hosts_deliveries_reach_the_exporters_of_its_service_defaults`, which gained one assertion: an exported activity of the source `Npgsql` whose parent span is the delivery's | Red without `NpgsqlSource` in the recipe's `AddSource(...)`: `Assert.Contains() Failure: Filter not matched in collection` at the new assertion, the Kit's assertion above it still passing. Observed in the green run with a temporary dump, which was not kept: under the Inventory delivery, the Receipt insert, EF Core's savepoint, the `stock_items` insert and the savepoint's release, each with its SQL in `db.query.text`; under Catalog's `CreateProductCommand`, the Outbox's inserts into `wolverine_incoming_envelopes` and `wolverine_outgoing_envelopes`; a `CONNECT` span for each physical connection opened. The commit is no command and had no span |
| An invalid Module setting fails the Host at start: `Inventory:ReconcileStock:Every` set to zero stops the Host with an `OptionsValidationException` that names the option | `RecurringCommandTests.A_host_whose_recurring_command_has_no_cadence_fails_to_start`, the existing test of #45 ([its section above](#wave-2-durable-recurring-system-work-ticket-45)) | No new run. ## Pre-Wave 3 public API changes, ticket #81

Ticket #81 of spec #74 (findings D16, D7, D18, A2, A4, A5, A6, A10 and V13): the public API changes the final assessment approved. They are the initiative's only intentional breaks, taken before the first public release because the Kit has no external consumer, so no alias or forwarder is kept. No namespace changed, no package or version pin was added, and no consumer's `using` line changes. `src/PouryaKit.Caching/HybridQueryCache.cs` was added to the brief's files for V13 by the session's decision of 2026-10-10.

WolverineFx declares no `IOutbox` (the clash check below) |
| D7: `Operation` has no public constructor; the Kit's registration builds it with the reading guard | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors; a search finds no construction of an `Operation` but `AddPouryaKitCqrs`'s | No test built one through the removed constructor, so none changed |
| D18: `PouryaKit.Domain` has no `ValueObject`; a value object is a plain record, which gives value equality, and a rule is checked with `Entity.CheckRule` | The build; nothing in the Kit, the Proving Sample, the templates or the tests derived from it | A removal: no red run. The sample's `ValueObjects` folders hold typed IDs and keep their names |

## Pre-Wave 3 proofs before fixes, ticket #82

Ticket #82 of spec #74 (findings T1, T2, T3 and P7): four open questions about runtime guarantees, each settled by a run before any fix ticket starts, so that a red result cannot change a fix ticket's scope midway. A red result counts as a confirmed problem only when its failing output repeats on a second run; none is fixed here. Environment of every run: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.401, Docker 29.8.1, `postgres:18.6` through Testcontainers, WolverineFx 6.48.3, `Microsoft.Extensions.Caching.Hybrid` 10.10.0, EF Core 10.0.12, Npgsql 10.0.3.

| Question | Proven by | Result | Red once, or the two red runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| T1, a runtime guarantee: does a tag eviction hold for a cached query whose handler read before the command committed and returns after the eviction? | A PostgreSQL test of `CachingTests`' shape, not kept in the tree: Catalog's `GetProduct` for the Actor ada, held after its Dapper read by a test probe of `IReadConnectionFactory` whose connection's disposal waits for the test, so the handler has read and not returned; `RenameProduct` then commits and evicts `product:{id}`; the hold is released and ada reads again | **Red.** The rename succeeds and the held read answers the old name, as it should; the read after it answers the old name too, from an entry stored after the eviction, which lives until it expires (5 minutes). `HybridCache` stamps an entry when its run starts and, when one of the entry's tags was evicted while the factory ran, stamps it again when the factory completes, so the eviction does not reach it: decompiled `Microsoft.Extensions.Caching.Hybrid` 10.10.0, `DefaultHybridCache.StampedeState<TState, T>.BackgroundFetchAsync`, `if (!Cache.IsValid(CacheItem))` followed by `CacheItem.UnsafeSetCreationTimestamp(...)` with the time the factory returned. The Kit's skill already states this as a limit ([caching.md](../.agents/skills/pourya-kit/references/caching.md#limits)); the README's "evicted once an Operation commits" does not. The orchestrator sizes the fix | Both runs failed at the last read, on 2026-10-09 at 22:49:50 and 22:50:06 UTC: `Assert.Equal() Failure: Strings differ`, `Expected: "Desk lamp"`, `Actual:   "Lamp"`. The test is a correct test of the README's claim, not of what the Kit's skill records, so it stays out of the tree with its probe. *Since ticket #93 (2026-10-10) the test and its probe are in the tree, the test with a joiner added, and pass: a read in flight across the commit stores nothing ([its section](#pre-wave-3-a-cached-read-in-flight-across-a-commit-stores-nothing-ticket-93)).* |
| T2: does a recurring command's cadence survive a release that changes its command's shape while an occurrence waits in the store? | `RecurringCommandTests.An_occurrence_stored_before_its_command_changed_shape_is_set_aside_and_logged_and_its_cadence_waits_for_the_next_host_start` | **The Host reports it; the cadence does not go on.** Inventory's `ReconcileStock`, hourly, on a database of its own: a Host stores the first occurrence and stops; the occurrence's stored body is rewritten through Wolverine's `EnvelopeSerializer` with the message an earlier shape wrote, its occurrence under a member since renamed; a restarted Host finds it waiting and stores none, and it is then made due. Wolverine cannot read it and sets it aside before the Kit's delivery runs (`HandlerPipeline.TryDeserializeEnvelope`, then `MoveToErrorQueue`): a dead letter at `local://inventory.reconcile-stock/` with `System.Text.Json.JsonException`, "JSON deserialization for type 'Inventory.StockItems.Features.ReconcileStock.ReconcileStockCommand' was missing required properties including: 'occurrence'.", and an Error entry of `Wolverine.Runtime.WolverineRuntime`, "Envelope Envelope #… from PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine to local://inventory.reconcile-stock/ was moved to the error queue", with that exception. No Receipt, no reconciliation, and no next occurrence until the next Host start, which stores the first one after now. That is not the guarantee recorded for a dead-lettered occurrence (the Wave 2 final review's package C, finding R2: the cadence goes on), which covers a failure of the occurrence's handler: an occurrence that no longer reads never reaches `DelayedCommandDelivery`. The orchestrator sizes the fix. *Since ticket #94 (2026-10-10) the limitation stands: no store API of WolverineFx 6.48.3 reads the bodies of one queue's waiting occurrences, so a starting Host does not check them, and the recovery is documented ([its section](#pre-wave-3-a-recurring-commands-cadence-after-an-occurrence-that-no-longer-reads-ticket-94)).* | Red once without `RespectRequiredConstructorParameters` in `UsePouryaKitWireJson`: the old body read with a default occurrence and ran, and the test failed waiting, `Not reached within 15 s: exists (select from wolverine.wolverine_dead_letters where received_at = 'local://inventory.reconcile-stock/')` |
| T3: does the Kit still need `UseEntityFrameworkCoreTransactions(TransactionMiddlewareMode.Eager)` in `UsePouryaKitWolverine(connectionName)`? | The whole suite with the line removed: `dotnet test PouryaKit.slnx` at `4bf4b82`, before any other change of the ticket | **No.** 830 passed, 0 failed, the suite's whole count, in 2m27s; `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx` 0 warnings. The line and its two `using` directives are removed. What it registered, and why the Outbox and the Kit's pre-built handlers use none of it, is [ADR 0004](adr/0004-commit-through-wolverine-ef-outbox.md)'s #82 amendment | A removal: the passing suite without it is the proof |
| P7: is a delayed command or a recurring occurrence due later than `InboxStaleTime` handed out again, and with what effect? | `RecurringCommandTests.A_delayed_command_and_an_occurrence_due_after_the_stale_time_and_handed_out_again_while_they_run_apply_one_effect_each_and_store_one_next_occurrence` | **Handed out again only when a recovery poll comes while an attempt runs; one effect each, and one next occurrence.** On a database of its own, with a 500 ms stale time: the Stub's `WriteKindChangedNoteCommand` declared as the delayed command `stub.stale-note`, due 2 s after its commit, and `ReconcileStock` every 2 s, the held occurrence being one an occurrence's commit stored, so due a cadence later. Each is held after its Receipt until a copy a recovery poll handed out waits on that Receipt (a `Lock` wait in `pg_stat_activity`), then released: one note and one Receipt for the delayed command, one reconciliation and one Receipt for the occurrence, each delivered at least twice, one occurrence waiting next, no dead letter. A delivery waiting in the store is owned by no node, and the release takes only owned rows (`BumpStaleIncomingEnvelopesOperation`, `owner_id != 0 and timestamp <= cutoff`, decompiled `Wolverine.RDBMS` 6.48.3). In three runs with a temporary dump, not kept, each of the 7 occurrences not held, due 1 to 2 s after it was stored, had one attempt, and each held one had two | Red once with the delivery ignoring a Receipt already recorded (`RunDelayedCommandHandler`): `Assert.Equal() Failure: Collections differ`, `Expected: [[1, 1, 1, 1]]`, `Actual:   [[2, 1, 2, 1]]`, two notes and two reconciliations |
| P7's sentence | `PouryaKitMessageStoreOptions.InboxStaleTime`'s summary no longer tells every Host to set it longer than every retry delay plus every attempt's run, which an hourly occurrence never meets under the 5-minute default. It now reads: "longer than every retry delay together plus every attempt's run, queue wait included (`PouryaKitMessagingOptions.RetryDelays`), it keeps a delivery sent at its commit from going stale, but a delayed command or a recurring occurrence due later than it, as an hourly one always is, runs stale from its first attempt and, held by no instance while it waits, is handed out again only when a recovery poll finds an attempt of it still running"; the summary's last sentence adds that the Receipt keeps a recurring command's next occurrence to one | | |
| Docs | The Kit's skill, `messaging.md` ("The stale-time rule", and under "Recurring commands" what T2 showed); [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md) (the message store's registration, no EF Core middleware mode, and the stale time); ADR 0004 (#82 amendment) and ADR 0006 (a dated note on the #44 amendment, whose "No test pins this" the P7 test now does) | | |
| Out of scope | | Any fix of T1 or T2, which the orchestrator sizes as their own work; the README's caching line, which T1 questions and no change of this ticket alters; a test that an unheld attempt never overlaps a recovery poll, which no setting can make certain | |

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since f836e9b` on the ticket's source (commit `eb15280`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.35`, the version its manifest records for that commit, with the Release runtime suite at 832 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. It ran in full: 4 of the 13 changed paths can reach a package, `src/PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine.EFCore/PouryaKitMessageStoreOptions.cs` among them.

After the review (two lows, both XML summaries that restated the replaced sentences: `AddPouryaKitRecurringCommand`'s "the cadence goes on" and `RetryDelays`' stale-time advice), the orchestrator's fix commit `214597a` reran the same command and printed the same PASS line at `0.1.0-preview.0.37`, with the Release runtime suite at 832 passed, 0 failed.

## Pre-Wave 3 option validation at start, ticket #83

Ticket #83 of spec #74 (finding Q1 and its implementer constraint): the four Kit option types registered with a bare `AddOptions<T>()` are validated at start, each with `Validate` and `ValidateOnStart()` chained at its registration, as `AddPouryaKitJwtBearer` validates the same two claims. An intentional behaviour change: a Host given one of the refused values no longer starts, and the `OptionsValidationException` names the option; before #83 it started and failed at the first request, or never visibly. Environment of every run: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.401, Docker 29.8.1, `postgres:18.6` through Testcontainers, WolverineFx 6.48.3.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| `PouryaKitWebOptions`: an `ActorClaim` that is null, empty or whitespace, or a `TenantClaim` that is empty or whitespace, stops the Host at `StartAsync` with the messages of the same two checks on `PouryaKitJwtBearerOptions`, naming `PouryaKitWebOptions`. Observed at start: the test's Host maps no Module endpoints; one that does fails earlier, in `MapPouryaKitModuleEndpoints`, the first read | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_blank_actor_or_tenant_claim_stops_the_host_at_start_naming_the_option`, five cases | With the Actor claim check removed, its three cases went red; with the tenant claim check removed, its two; with only `ValidateOnStart()` removed, all five |
| `PouryaKitTrustedPeerOptions`: a blank peer, empty or spaces, bound from configuration beside a valid one as the Hosts bind their peers, stops the Host at `StartAsync`. The type has no Kit registration, so its validation and `ValidateOnStart()` are in `AddPouryaKitWeb`, through `AddOptions<T>()`, which leaves the Hosts' `Configure<T>` binding as it was | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_blank_trusted_peer_stops_the_host_at_start_naming_the_option`, two cases; `TrustedPeerTests`, the whole class, for the binding | With the check removed, both went red; with only its `ValidateOnStart()` removed, both |
| A null `TenantClaim` (read no tenant) and an empty peer list stay valid | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_null_tenant_claim_and_an_empty_peer_list_let_the_host_start` | Red with the tenant check refusing null, and with the peer check refusing an empty list (that variant also turned eight other tests of the class red, whose Hosts configure no peer) |
| `PouryaKitMessagingOptions`: a null `RetryDelays`, or one holding a negative delay, stops the Host at `StartAsync`. Observed at the first read: Wolverine's options, built while `StartAsync` resolves the hosted services, read the delays before the start-time validation runs | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_null_empty_or_negative_retry_schedule_stops_the_host_at_start_naming_the_option`, three cases; since ticket #95 (2026-10-10) four, the empty schedule added and the theory renamed to say so ([its section](#pre-wave-3-an-empty-retry-schedule-refused-at-start-ticket-95)) | With the null check removed, the null case went red; with the negative check removed, both negative cases. With only `ValidateOnStart()` removed, all three still passed: the first read fails the start before it, so the validation is the switch that proves the guarantee here, and `ValidateOnStart()` keeps it if that read ever moves later |
| A zero-delay schedule stays valid | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_zero_delay_retry_schedule_lets_the_host_start` | Red with the check refusing a zero delay |
| `PouryaKitMessageStoreOptions`: an `InboxStaleTime` of zero or less stops the sample's Host at `StartAsync`, at the first read, when Wolverine's options are built, before the store is reached | `MisconfiguredHostTests.An_inbox_stale_time_of_zero_or_less_stops_the_host_at_start_naming_the_option`, two cases | With the check removed, both went red; with only `ValidateOnStart()` removed, both still passed, as for the retry schedule |
| Deviation: the ticket names "an empty or zero-delay retry schedule" as a positive case, but an empty schedule was never valid. On the unmodified tree it fails `StartAsync` with Wolverine's `InvalidOperationException`, "You must specify at least one delay time", thrown by `FailureActions.ScheduleRetry` when `src/PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine/PouryaKitWolverineExtension.cs` passes it the delays. The session decided on 2026-10-10: the validation refuses only null and negative delays, the positive case is the zero delay, and the option's docs say an empty schedule fails at start | The positive test with an empty schedule, run once on the unmodified tree and then removed | Open for the owner: an empty schedule meaning "no retry, set aside at once" in the Wolverine extension, or refused by the Kit's validation with its own message |
| Docs | The XML summaries of the four option types; the Kit's skill, `web.md` (the claims, the trusted peers) and `messaging.md` (the retries, the message store); [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md) (one bullet each in Web, Messaging.Wolverine and Messaging.Wolverine.EFCore) | |

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since ff60843` on the ticket's source (commit `d033e8d`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.37`, the version its manifest records for that commit, with the Release runtime suite at 853 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. It ran in full: the three changed registrations and four option types are in packed packages, and the sample's Host and both generated Hosts, whose option values are all valid, still start.

After the review (two lows: the `RetryDelays` summary and the skill overclaimed that an empty schedule fails naming the option, and `AddPouryaKitWeb`'s summary omitted its two validations), the orchestrator's fix commit `1e3f100` reran the same command and printed the same PASS line at `0.1.0-preview.0.39`.

## Pre-Wave 3 Cqrs, Dapper and Observability fixes, ticket #84

Ticket #84 of spec #74 (findings V1, V2, V8, Q8 and Q9's Cqrs and Dapper sites): the dispatch path's telemetry, its allocation when nothing listens and two lookups, fixed where the final assessment confirmed them, and V1 measured only. No public API changed, no package or version pin was added, and V1 changed no code.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| V2: `pouryakit.operation.duration` declares ASP.NET Core's second-scale bucket boundaries for `http.server.request.duration` (`dotnet/aspnetcore@7387de9`, tag `v10.0.0`, `MetricsConstants.ShortSecondsBucketBoundaries`) as its `InstrumentAdvice<double>`, and the OpenTelemetry SDK exports them with no view | `HostRecipeTests.Inventory_hosts_deliveries_reach_the_exporters_of_its_service_defaults`, which gained one assertion: every exported point of the histogram has the fourteen boundaries, 0.005 to 10, read from `MetricPoint.GetHistogramBuckets()` | Red without the advice: `Assert.All() Failure: 6 out of 6 items in the collection did not pass`, `Actual: List<double> [0, 5, 10, 25, 50, ···]`, the SDK's millisecond defaults |
| V8: the dispatcher's log scope carries the Operation's `OperationId`, `CorrelationId` and `Module` beside its type name, so its logs join their request without a tracing exporter | `HostRecipeTests.With_serilog_the_kits_logs_reach_a_sink_the_host_registers`, which gained one assertion: an entry the `Dispatcher` logged in `Inventory.Host` carries `Deliver`, the delivery's own `OperationId` (from `DeliveryProbes.ContextsOf`), the correlation of the `CreateProduct` it continues and `InventoryModule`. Serilog's logger provider turns the scope's values into the entry's properties, so the existing sink is the capture | Red with the scope given nulls for the three values: `Assert.Contains() Failure: Item not found in collection`, every tuple `00000000-…, 00000000-…, null` |
| Q8: the fast path keeps the two-part predicate: it is taken only with no activity listener and the histogram disabled, so a Host with either listener alone still observes | `ObservabilityTests.With_only_a_meter_listener_a_command_still_records_its_duration`, `ObservabilityTests.With_only_an_activity_listener_a_command_is_still_an_activity`; `RecordedTelemetry` can leave out either listener | Red with `\|\|` turned into `&&`: both new cases failed (`Filter not matched in collection`), the class's other five passed |
| Q8: with nothing listening, the wrapper returns the inner dispatcher's own task and allocates nothing: its delegates are static, so no closure, delegate or state machine is created before the check | `ObservedDispatcherTests.With_nothing_listening_the_observed_dispatcher_returns_the_dispatchers_own_task_and_allocates_nothing` (`PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests`, which now references `PouryaKit.Observability`): a scope's observed dispatcher around a stand-in that returns completed tasks, warmed with 1,000 rounds, then `GC.GetAllocatedBytesForCurrentThread()` around 10,000 rounds of a command, a command with a response and a query | Red on the code before the change: `Actual: Tuple (False, False, False, 15840000)` in Debug and `(False, False, False, 5280000)` in Release, 528 and 176 bytes per dispatch; after it 0 in both, with the inner task returned as it is |
| Q9: `PageSql.DbTypes` is a `FrozenDictionary`, as `PageDeclaration`'s and `FilterField`'s lookups are; `FilterOperatorNames.TryParseName` reads one cached `Enum.GetValues` array and keeps its loop, so a null name still answers false | `PageTests.Each_operator_has_one_short_name_that_parses_back_to_it`, which gained the null case; `DapperTests` and `PagingTests` through the full run | The null case is red with `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(name)` added to `TryParseName`; the frozen lookup answers as before, a refactor with no red run |
| The Kit builds and the ticket's suites pass | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors. `ModularMonolith.Tests` 571 passed, 0 failed; `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests` 46; `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 102; `check-docs.py` and `check-ai-setup.py` 0 failures | |
| Docs | The Kit's skill (`observability.md`: the one check, either listener alone, the histogram's boundaries, the log scope; `commands-queries-validation.md`: the logging step's scope), [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md) (the pipeline's logging step, the wrapper's fast path and the histogram's advice), `README.md`'s observability line, and the provenance record of `PouryaKit.Observability` (the boundaries' source, adoption `pattern`: the values only) | |
| Out of scope | | No change from V1's numbers (below). Q9's other two sites, `Subscriptions.ReservedQueues` and the header-value check, belong to other tickets of the spec |

### V1: the measurement

`EnterQuery` and `EnterChecks` each open a reading boundary, and each captures every tracked entry (`src/PouryaKit.Cqrs/Pipeline/Dispatcher.cs`, `src/PouryaKit.Persistence.EFCore/UnitOfWork/TrackedState.cs`). Both stay (ADR 0003): this ticket only sizes them. A throwaway console project outside the repository, built in Release against this branch, measured two things:

- **The second capture per nested query.** A command handler attaches N entities of five scalar properties to a DbContext that never opens a connection, then times 100 nested queries whose handler returns at once. The guard is `EfUnitOfWork`'s (`src/PouryaKit.Persistence.EFCore/UnitOfWork/EfUnitOfWork.cs`, `TrackedState.Capture(dbContext, includeQueryLoads: true)` compared when the boundary closes); a switch leaves out the checks boundary's capture, which is what removing it would save. 41 alternating rounds of each shape, medians.
- **The DbContext a Dapper-only read constructs.** In the Proving Sample's Host, built and migrated against `postgres:18.6` but not started, Catalog's `ListProducts` through the dispatcher (an empty first Page: its select and count), 1,001 reads; and 15 rounds of 2,000 scopes of Catalog's Operation, timed and counted with `GC.GetAllocatedBytesForCurrentThread()` with nothing more, with its `IReadingStateGuard` resolved, and with its two boundaries opened and closed.

```csharp
// The guard: EfUnitOfWork's, or with the checks boundary's capture left out.
public IDisposable OnlyReading(string step) =>
    SkipChecksCapture && step == "An authorizer or a validator"
        ? NoCapture.Instance
        : new ReadingState(TrackedState.Capture(db, includeQueryLoads: true), step);

// The command's handler: N tracked entities, then 100 timed nested queries.
db.Items.AttachRange(Item.Many(command.Entities));
var started = Stopwatch.GetTimestamp();
for (var i = 0; i < command.Queries; i++)
{
    await dispatcher.QueryAsync(new NothingQuery(), cancellationToken);
}

return Stopwatch.GetElapsedTime(started).Ticks;

// A Dapper-only read's guard, per scope: step 0 opens the scope only.
using var scope = host.Services.CreateScope();
scope.ServiceProvider.BeginOperation(context, typeof(CatalogModule));
var guard = scope.ServiceProvider.GetRequiredService<IReadingStateGuard>(); // step 1
guard.OnlyReading("A query").Dispose(); // step 2
guard.OnlyReading("An authorizer or a validator").Dispose();
```

| N tracked entities | One boundary, capture and comparison | A nested query with both captures | With the outer capture only | The second capture | Its share |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0.3 to 0.9 µs | 1.2 to 2.9 µs | 0.9 to 1.9 µs | 0.2 to 1.0 µs | 19 to 35 % |
| 10 | 8.3 to 9.1 µs | 18.5 to 24.7 µs | 8.5 to 13.4 µs | 10.0 to 11.3 µs | 46 to 54 % |
| 100 | 78 to 79 µs | 162 to 171 µs | 81 to 84 µs | 81 to 86 µs | 50 to 51 % |
| 1000 | 1,087 to 1,162 µs | 2,060 to 2,073 µs | 893 to 905 µs | 1,155 to 1,180 µs | 56 to 57 % |

A Dapper-only read: the read took 877 to 1,194 µs (median); its guard added 21.0 to 21.6 µs and 59,300 to 59,450 bytes per read, 1.8 to 2.4 % of the read: resolving it, which constructs Catalog's DbContext and `EfUnitOfWork`, 20.6 to 21.6 µs and 58,140 bytes, and its two captures of the empty change tracker 0.07 to 0.46 µs and about 1,200 bytes.

The ranges are two runs on 2026-10-10: macOS 27.0.1 (Darwin 27.0.0), Apple silicon, 18 cores, .NET 10.0.12 (SDK 10.0.401), Docker 29.8.1. Other agents' test suites ran beside them (a load average of 6 to 8; in the first run three `postgres:18.6`, a `rabbitmq:4.3.6` and a `redis:8.10.1` container of another suite), so the absolute times are indicative; with 10 or more tracked entities the shares agree between the runs. What they say: the second capture costs about 1 µs per tracked entity per nested query, half of the query's own cost once anything is tracked, and a Dapper-only read pays about 21 µs and 58 KB to construct a DbContext it never uses. Neither number changes code here; the orchestrator sizes any change, and none may remove a comparison.

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since ff60843` on the ticket's source (commit `d9d57da`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.38`, the version its manifest records for that commit, with the Release runtime suite at 842 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. It ran in full: 10 of the 22 changed paths can reach a package, `src/PouryaKit.Cqrs/Paging/FilterOperatorNames.cs` among them. Its first run on the same commit failed in that suite at 841 passed, 1 failed: `RecurringCommandTests.An_occurrence_stored_before_its_command_changed_shape_is_set_aside_and_logged_and_its_cadence_waits_for_the_next_host_start` could not start its Host, `53300: sorry, too many clients already` from the test container's PostgreSQL, while other agents' suites ran on the same Docker host; the rerun, with nothing changed, passed.

## Pre-Wave 3 Messaging and Persistence fixes, ticket #85

Ticket #85 of spec #74 (findings P1, Q5, V10, V11, V12, Q13, Q9 and D15): one high, a published Snapshot that could differ from the row its Operation committed, against ADR 0003, and seven smaller fixes of the delivery and commit paths. No public member changed, no package or version pin was added. `src/PouryaKit.Messaging/ISnapshotMapper.cs` was added to the brief's files for P1's XML summary by the session's decision of 2026-10-10. Every proof runs against PostgreSQL in Testcontainers, `CrossProcessTracingTests` against RabbitMQ too.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| P1: a commit that changes an aggregate with a declared Snapshot is refused, naming the property, when the database generates a value of the aggregate itself: the Stub's `Ticket`, whose `Serial` has a default value SQL; nothing commits and nothing is published | `SnapshotTests.A_snapshot_of_an_aggregate_whose_database_generates_a_value_it_maps_fails_the_operation_that_saves_it` | Red with the refusal off: `Expected: ["printed by the database"]`, `Actual: [null]`, the row committed the database's serial and the Snapshot published none |
| P1: the same when the value is in a part the aggregate owns and the mapper reads: the Stub's `Drawer`, whose owned `Knob.Label` is a computed column, so the refusal covers the aggregate's mapped graph (the aggregate, the types it owns, the children it holds through a navigation, their derived types), not its root alone | `SnapshotTests.A_snapshot_of_an_aggregate_whose_database_generates_a_value_of_an_owned_part_it_maps_fails_the_operation_that_saves_it` | Red with the refusal off: `Expected: ["BRASS"]`, `Actual: [null]`. Red alone, the root's test green, with the walk kept to the root |
| P1, review finding F2: a value a client-side value generator sets for good when the entity is added is not refused, since the database writes none of it: the Stub's `Badge`, whose `Code` is generated on add by EF's `GuidValueGenerator`, commits and its Snapshot carries the code committed. A non-key property generated on add only is refused unless its mapping names a generator, the one EF's `IValueGeneratorSelector` returns, whose values are not temporary | `SnapshotTests.A_snapshot_of_an_aggregate_whose_value_a_client_side_generator_sets_carries_the_value_committed` | Red with that exception taken out: `System.InvalidOperationException : A Snapshot of Badge is declared, but Badge.Code is generated by the database when it is saved`. Both regressions above went red again with the refusal off after the fix, and the owned-part one alone with the walk kept to the root |
| P1: the existing Snapshot tests stay green; the Kit's own conventions (Guid version 7 keys, Kit-set version and audit fields, a TPH discriminator) meet no refusal | `SnapshotTests`: 33 passed | |
| V11: the key-space check no longer walks the EF model at each commit: the model's aggregate types and their roots are read once per DbContext type, in a static cache as `SnapshotMappers.Mappings` is; the throw stays | `SnapshotTests.A_snapshot_declared_for_aggregates_of_two_mapped_hierarchies_fails_the_operation_that_would_publish_it` | By design, not a red run: the verdict itself is not cached. That test's Host declares one Snapshot more over the same Module and DbContext as the fixture Host, and a Host's declarations are its own, so a verdict per DbContext type and Module shared across Hosts would skip it; the check runs at each commit over the cached read. *Corrected on 2026-10-10 by ticket #92 (final review R1): the read is cached once per model, the `IModel` instance the DbContext exposes, not once per DbContext type, since one DbContext type can carry several models (`UseModel`, a model cache key per tenant) and a read cached by type gave a second model the first one's roots and generated properties. `SnapshotTests.A_snapshot_is_refused_by_the_model_its_dbcontext_uses_when_one_dbcontext_type_has_two_models` proves it with no database, red with the type key: the second model's refusal did not fire (`No exception was thrown`), and, with the test's two halves swapped, the plain model after the stamped one was refused.* |
| Q5: Messaging reads the Actor kind header with Web's name switch: exactly `Person` or `System`, anything else the Kit's `InvalidOperationException` | `OperationContextTests.An_actor_kind_header_that_is_not_exactly_person_or_system_is_refused` (`"1"`, `"Person, System"`, `"Robot"`, `" Person"`, `"System "`); `OperationContextTests.A_person_and_a_system_actor_continue_as_themselves`; `OperationContextTests.An_undefined_numeric_actor_kind_cannot_continue_as_a_system_actor` unchanged in what it asserts | Red with the header parsed as an enum again: four cases accepted (`No exception was thrown`), and `"Robot"` threw `System.ArgumentException` |
| V10: a delivery's Operation activity carries `pouryakit.subscription`, the subscription's name, set by `DeliverHandler`; the duration histogram keeps its tags | `CrossProcessTracingTests.A_product_created_in_the_catalog_host_and_stocked_by_inventory_host_is_one_trace_with_the_upstream_tracestate`, both cases: each attempt of `inventory.product-created` and the delivery of `catalog.product-created.publish-product-created` | Red with the tag removed: `Expected: ("InventoryModule", "inventory.product-created", …)`, `Actual: ("InventoryModule", null, …)`, in both cases |
| V10: a delayed or recurring command's run carries its declared name the same way, set by `RunDelayedCommandHandler` | `DelayedCommandTests.A_delayed_commands_run_is_an_operation_tagged_with_its_name` | Red with the tag removed: `Expected: stub.delayed-note`, `Actual: null` |
| Q9: `Subscriptions.ReservedQueues` is a frozen set that keeps its ordinal-ignore-case comparer | `DelayedCommandTests.A_delayed_command_named_after_a_queue_wolverine_keeps_for_itself_is_refused` (`scheduled`, and the mixed-case `ScheDuled`) | Red with `StringComparer.Ordinal`: `ScheDuled` was accepted |
| V12: the idempotency key cleanup cleans the DbContexts `AddPouryaKitEfCore` recorded as `DbContextConnection`, not EF's non-generic options registrations | `IdempotencyRetentionTests`: 9 passed | A change of lookup: the existing tests are the proof |
| Q13: the rollback's `CancellationToken.None` states why: a rollback completes whatever the caller cancelled | The comment in `EfUnitOfWork.RunAsync` | |
| D15: the idempotency store, its cleanup and its table are PostgreSQL-only, as the Receipts are; `AddPouryaKitEfCore` installs the cleanup in every Host, and it walks every Unit of Work DbContext, probing each with `to_regclass` and returning silently for a Module with no `[Idempotent]` command | The XML docs of `AddPouryaKitEfCore`, `CreateIdempotencyTable` and `PouryaKitIdempotencyOptions`; `persistence.md`; one line in the plan's section C and one in [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md) | No code change |
| The Kit builds and the suites pass | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors. `ModularMonolith.Tests` 580 passed, 0 failed (11 new: the two P1 tests and the client-side generator's, the six Actor kind cases, the delayed command's tag and the mixed-case reserved name); `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 102 passed | |
| Docs | The Kit's skill (`messaging.md`'s rules of a mapper and the Host's checks, `observability.md`, `persistence.md`), the XML summaries of `ISnapshotMapper`, `PublishSnapshot` and `DbContextConnection`, [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md) (the delivery step, the Snapshot step, the cleanup's lookup, D15), the plan's section C (D15), a dated amendment of [ADR 0003](adr/0003-single-commit-owner.md) and a dated note in [ADR 0006](adr/0006-independent-delivery-through-a-routed-queue-per-subscription.md)'s Wave 1 amendment | | |
| Out of scope | | A child entity whose own key the database generates is still allowed (the brief keeps keys out of the refusal); a mapper that read such a key would publish a temporary value. The databases health check and `HostFixture` still find DbContexts through `DbContextOptions`, a concern of their own |

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since ff60843` on the ticket's source (commit `08bf968`, before this section was added) printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.37`, the version its manifest records for that commit, with the Release runtime suite at 849 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. ## Pre-Wave 3 the Web and shared JSON fixes, ticket #86

Ticket #86 of spec #74 (findings P8, V9 under the owner's decision D9, V14 and Q9's Web site): three fixes at the HTTP and wire trust boundary and one idiom. V9 and V14 are intentional behaviour changes. No public API changed: the one new type, `KitBadRequestException`, is internal to `PouryaKit.Web`. No package or version pin was added. The XML summary of `UsePouryaKitWolverine` in `src/PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine/WolverineHostApplicationBuilderExtensions.cs`, outside the brief's files, was changed by one sentence on the session's grant of 2026-10-10, because it restates the wire's reading rule.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| P8: a capitalised `Filter[...]` key filters the Page, as `page`, `size`, `sort` and `direction` are already read ignoring case through `IQueryCollection` | `PagingTests.A_filter_key_whose_prefix_is_capitalised_filters_the_page` | Red with the prefix compared `Ordinal`: the unfiltered Page came back, `Collections differ at index 1`, `Expected: "Floor lamp"`, `Actual: "Oak desk"` |
| V9 over HTTP: a body that names a member twice, `name` twice or `name` and `Name`, is a body that cannot be read: 400 as a problem response, without `errors` and, being ASP.NET Core's own refusal, without `detail`, and nothing is saved | `WebTests.A_body_that_names_a_member_twice_answers_400_as_a_problem_response_and_nothing_is_saved` (two cases) | Red with `AllowDuplicateProperties` at its default: both cases answered 200 (`Expected: BadRequest`, `Actual: OK`), the body read with its last value |
| V9 on the wire: a broker envelope whose body names a member of its contract twice is dead-lettered at the receiving Host's queue with `Duplicate property 'name' encountered during deserialization of type 'ModularMonolith.Tests.ShapeV1'.`, and no subscription receives a copy | `StrictWireReadingTests.A_body_that_names_a_member_of_its_contract_twice_is_dead_lettered_at_the_host_queue_with_the_error_and_never_handled` (two Hosts on RabbitMQ; the publishing contract writes the name again from `[JsonExtensionData]`, which System.Text.Json writes without checking names) | The same mutation: the handler received the envelope (`Assert.DoesNotContain() Failure: Filter matched in collection`). One line covers both sides because `UsePouryaKitWireJson` applies `UsePouryaKitJson` first |
| V14: the Kit's own 400 reasons reach the caller as the problem's `detail`, word for word: a page query string (six cases), a missing or unreadable `Idempotency-Key` (three cases on POST, and on PUT and DELETE), a trusted peer's incomplete or unreadable context headers (five cases) | `PagingTests.A_page_query_string_that_cannot_be_read_answers_400_as_a_problem_response`; `IdempotencyTests.A_request_without_a_readable_idempotency_key_answers_400_as_a_problem_response_and_creates_nothing`; `IdempotencyTests.An_idempotent_put_or_delete_without_a_key_answers_400_and_with_one_replays_the_first_response`; `TrustedPeerTests.A_trusted_peer_whose_context_headers_are_incomplete_or_unreadable_is_refused_with_400` | Red with the refusing sites throwing ASP.NET Core's bare `BadHttpRequestException`, as before the subclass: all 16 cases failed, each with `KeyNotFoundException` at `GetProperty("detail")` |

## Pre-Wave 3 the registration naming rule and private field naming, ticket #88

Ticket #88 of spec #74, items C1 and C2 with decision D17 (option a): the registration naming rule is written down, and private field naming is enforced by the build. No behaviour, public member, registration name, package or version pin changed: the renames touch eleven private fields and their uses, nothing else. Environment of every run: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.401, Docker 29.8.1, `postgres:18.6` through Testcontainers, WolverineFx 6.48.3.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| The registration naming rule: `Use*` mirrors the verb of the framework call it wraps, applies a representation, or configures existing behaviour, so `UsePouryaKitTestDelivery` keeps its name; `Add*` adds a Capability's services; `Map*` maps; the Host builder is the receiver only when the call needs it. The one exception, by D17, is the Module's messaging vocabulary of ADR 0006: `Publish<T>`, `Receive<T>`, `Subscribe<T, THandler>`, `PublishSnapshot` and `ReceiveSnapshot` keep their names in the Composition Root, beside `AddPouryaKitDelayedCommand` and `AddPouryaKitRecurringCommand` | The "Registration names" line of `.claude/rules/kit-code.md`, under "Design" | A judgement call the reviewer carries; no registration was renamed |
| A private field that breaks the naming rule fails the build: PascalCase for `const` and `static readonly` fields, `_camelCase` for every other private field, mutable statics included, whether `private` is written or implied. `.editorconfig`'s `[*.cs]` section gives three symbol groups of `private` fields, one with the `const` modifier, one with `static` and `readonly` and one with none, and `dotnet_diagnostic.IDE1006.severity = warning`, which the build reads; a naming rule's own `severity`, also `warning`, reaches only the IDE. Rules apply by specificity, so the two PascalCase groups win over the bare one | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors after the renames below | With the rule added and before any rename, the build failed with 4 errors, all IDE1006 on Core's two fields, each also reported by `Offending.Core`, which compiles Core's sources; the 7 projects that do not reference Core built, and the other 34, the four holding the other nine fields among them, were skipped. Built with warnings not as errors (`--no-incremental -p:TreatWarningsAsErrors=false`), all 43 projects compiled and reported 13 IDE1006 warnings and nothing else: the eleven fields below, the eleven the findings counted, and Core's two again in `Offending.Core` |
| A mutable static and an implied-`private` field are held to `_camelCase`; a `static readonly` or a `const` field is held to PascalCase | A throwaway class `NamingProbe` in `PouryaKit.Core.Tests`, built with `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx` and then deleted. Each field was read and written, so no unused-field warning joined the output | The build failed with exactly 5 errors, one per wrong shape: `static int mutableStatic;` (line 5) and `int impliedPrivate;` (line 6) gave `Missing prefix: '_'`; `private static readonly int _staticReadonly = 1;` (7) gave `Prefix '_' is not expected`; `private const int constant = 1;` (8) gave `These words must begin with upper case characters: constant`; `private static int MutableStaticPascal;` (9) gave `Missing prefix: '_'`. The four valid shapes beside them, a `static readonly` and a `const` field in PascalCase and a mutable static and an instance field in `_camelCase`, gave none |
| Nothing else changed behaviour | `dotnet test PouryaKit.slnx`: 885 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (`ModularMonolith.Tests` 2m35s) | |
| The templates | No file under `src/PouryaKit.Templates/content` is an `.editorconfig`, and the generated solutions' shared props set no `EnforceCodeStyleInBuild` ([ADR 0010](adr/0010-one-namespace-per-kit-package.md) leaves them out of the namespace check the same way), so there is no template copy of the rule to keep in step. The sample's business code the templates pack builds under the rule here | |
| Docs and AI guidance | The "Registration names" line of `.claude/rules/kit-code.md`; the comment over the rule in `.editorconfig`. The rule file's "Current idioms" line lists the C# 14 and .NET 10 idioms the build enforces and is unchanged; the Kit's skill, `docs/architecture/` and the top-level docs state no field or registration naming convention | |
| Deviation | In the five constructor assignments of `SynchronizationContextScope` and `Pbkdf2PasswordHasher` where the field and its parameter shared a name, `this.previous = previous;` became `_previous = previous;`: the `this.` qualifier only told the field from the parameter, and no other file in the repository writes `this._` | |

The eleven fields, renamed with their uses only:

| Field | Old name | New name |
|---|---|---|
| `src/PouryaKit.Core/SynchronizationContextScope.cs:15` | `previous` | `_previous` |
| `src/PouryaKit.Core/SynchronizationContextScope.cs:16` | `entered` | `_entered` |
| `src/PouryaKit.Security/Pbkdf2PasswordHasher.cs:22` | `algorithm` | `_algorithm` |
| `src/PouryaKit.Security/Pbkdf2PasswordHasher.cs:23` | `iterations` | `_iterations` |
| `src/PouryaKit.Security/Pbkdf2PasswordHasher.cs:24` | `saltSize` | `_saltSize` |
| `src/PouryaKit.Security/Pbkdf2PasswordHasher.cs:25` | `keySize` | `_keySize` |
| `src/PouryaKit.Testing/StoredDeliveries.cs:16` | `quotedSchema` | `_quotedSchema` |
| `tests/ModularMonolith.Tests/HostRecipeTests.cs:40` | `downstream` | `_downstream` |
| `tests/ModularMonolith.Tests/OutgoingHttpTests.cs:27` | `downstream` | `_downstream` |
| `tests/PouryaKit.Core.Tests/PredicateBuilderTests.cs:10` | `rightEvaluations` | `_rightEvaluations` |
| `tests/PouryaKit.Core.Tests/SynchronizationContextScopeTests.cs:7` | `prior` | `_prior` |

## Pre-Wave 3 Capability docs, ADR 0006 and repository hygiene, ticket #90

Ticket #90 of spec #74 (findings P9, P10, V4, V5 with D10, D1, D2, D3, D11 and V17): the docs describe the code eighteen tickets changed, each fact kept in several copies has one owner, and two hygiene moves are made. No Kit code under `src/` changed. Beside the docs: a dated amendment and a dated note in ADR 0006, the CI workflow's `publication-checks` job removed, the publication test moved, and one pin removed from `Directory.Packages.props`. Each change to the plan, an ADR or an earlier row of this record is a dated note beside the text it corrects, which stays readable as history. This section sits between #89's and #93's, since `check-docs.py` keeps the Pre-Wave 3 sections in ticket order.

| What | Where | Evidence |
|---|---|---|

## Pre-Wave 3 a cached read in flight across a commit stores nothing, ticket #93

Ticket #93 of spec #74 settles T1 of [#82's section](#pre-wave-3-proofs-before-fixes-ticket-82), under the owner's decision of 2026-10-10: it is a correctness issue, and the fix is the smallest reliable one that keeps performance and the existing cache guarantees. The defect: a cached query whose handler read before a command committed, and returned after that command's tag eviction, stored its pre-commit answer until it expired (5 minutes for the sample's `GetProduct`). `HybridCache` 10.10.0 stamps an entry when its run starts. When one of the entry's tags was evicted while the factory ran, it stamps the entry again once the factory returns (`dotnet/extensions@52cffc2f91 src/Libraries/Microsoft.Extensions.Caching.Hybrid/Internal/DefaultHybridCache.StampedeStateT.cs`).

The fix lives in `HybridQueryCache` alone. It keeps an in-process record of this Host's handler runs in flight: a `ConcurrentDictionary` used as a set, each entry a private `RunningRead` holding the run's tags and an `Evicted` flag. A run enters the record when its factory starts, before the handler reads, and leaves it in the factory's `finally`. `EvictAsync` first marks every run in flight on one of the evicted tags, and every run when they include `HybridCache`'s wildcard tag `*`, which evicts every entry; then it evicts tag by tag as before. Once the handler has returned, its answer is serialized and the reading boundary closed, the factory checks its mark last. A marked run throws the existing private `NotStored`, which now carries either the failure's errors or the serialized answer. `HybridCache` therefore stores nothing, in memory or in a distributed cache. Every caller that shared the run catches it and reads that answer back, as from an entry, and the next read runs the handler again.

This is shape c1 of the ticket. A cache hit never runs the factory, so it pays nothing. Shape c2 would need the run's record outside the factory, which every hit would pay for. No lock, timer or second cache layer is involved. `IQueryCache`, the key, the tags, `HybridCacheEntryOptions` and its flags, and every package are unchanged. Environment of every run: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.401 (runtime 10.0.12), Docker 29.8.1, `postgres:18.6` and `redis:8.10.1` through Testcontainers, `Microsoft.Extensions.Caching.Hybrid` 10.10.0, WolverineFx 6.48.3.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| On one Host, a read that began before a commit and ended after its eviction leaves no entry. Catalog's `GetProduct` for ada is held after its Dapper read. The hold is #82's `ReadProbe`, restored into `Fixtures/` and installed in `AppFixture.ComposeHost` after `DeliveryProbes`. `RenameProduct` commits and evicts `product:{id}` while the read is held. Released, the held read answers `Lamp` and the next read answers `Desk lamp` | `CachingTests.A_read_that_began_before_a_committed_rename_and_ended_after_its_eviction_leaves_no_entry_of_the_old_name` | Red on the tip `b644fe9`. The test was restored verbatim from #82 and the Kit was left untouched, on 2026-10-10 at about 08:39:50 UTC. It failed at the last read with `Assert.Equal() Failure: Strings differ`, `Expected: "Desk lamp"`, `Actual:   "Lamp"`, as #82's two runs did on 2026-10-09 at 22:49:50 and 22:50:06 UTC. Red once with the fix's check removed (the `if (read.Evicted)` throw): the same assertion failed, and the class's other 17 tests passed. The test as kept, with the joiner, ran both ways again: on the tip's `HybridQueryCache.cs` and with the check removed, each failed the same way at the last read, `CachingTests.cs:259` |
| Every caller that shared a refused run receives its answer, not an exception. A second read for ada joins the held run before the rename, which `HybridCache`'s `StampedeJoin` event shows. It answers `Lamp` | The same test, its assertion on the joiner | Red with the refusal thrown as an `InvalidOperationException`, an exception the cache's catch does not handle and the trap the brief names.
