### What remains

- **The window in the committing Host.** The Kit checks the mark as the factory's last statement. `HybridCache` then checks the entry's tags (`IsValid`) and stores it (`SetL1`) in the same continuation, `BackgroundFetchAsync`, after the factory's `finally` and the await's return, with `ConfigureAwait(false)` and no queueing. Consider an eviction whose mark lands after the Kit's check and whose `RemoveByTagAsync` lands before `IsValid`. The entry is then stamped again and stored, as before the fix, until it expires. If its `RemoveByTagAsync` lands after `IsValid` instead, the entry keeps the run's start stamp, so the next read finds it invalid. Marking before evicting narrows this to the time between the check and `IsValid`, a few synchronous statements: microseconds, where the defect's window was the handler's whole run. No run hit it.
- **Another Host.** Its runs are not in this Host's record, so an eviction committed here does not mark them. As before, that Host may store an answer read before the commit and keep it until it expires, beside the copies the existing limit already covers.
- **A caller that joins after the commit.** It receives the run's answer, read before the commit, for that one call, because `RemoveByTagAsync` leaves runs in flight alone. The run stores nothing.

### The per-read cost

A throwaway console project outside the repository, built in Release against this worktree's `PouryaKit.Caching`, measured the cost. It composes `AddPouryaKitCaching()` with `AddLogging()` and resolves `IQueryCache`. It calls `QueryAsync` with a cacheable query of its own whose handler returns a completed success at once, in two shapes, each 21 rounds of 10,000 reads after a warm-up of 2,000. The first shape is a warmed hit: one entry read again and again. The second is a miss: distinct keys made before the clock starts, each read once, so each runs the factory. Each round is timed with `Stopwatch.GetTimestamp()` and weighed with `GC.GetAllocatedBytesForCurrentThread()`, as #84's Q8 was. The build of the tip and the build of the change ran alternately, four times each, under the same load.

| Per read | Tip `b644fe9` | With the change |
|---|---|---|
| Hit, median of a run's rounds | 1,170 to 1,188 ns | 1,143 to 1,216 ns |
| Hit, allocated | 1,024 bytes | 1,024 bytes |
| Miss, median of a run's rounds | 3,263 to 3,605 ns | 3,485 to 3,662 ns |
| Miss, allocated | 1,784.1 bytes | 1,872.1 bytes |

The ranges span four runs each, on 2026-10-10 (load average 5 to 6, with other agents' suites and containers running beside them), so the absolute times are indicative. What they say: a hit costs the same before and after, in time and in bytes, because a hit never runs the factory. A miss allocates 88 bytes more, which is the registration itself: the `RunningRead` (32 bytes), the `ConcurrentDictionary` node that holds it (40 bytes), and the two more references in the run's state, which `HybridCache` keeps in its stampede state (16 bytes). Its time is within the miss's noise. A refused run costs the next read one more handler run. What a commit pays is below.

### The per-commit cost

The ticket's item 5 asks for one lookup per evicted tag. The record is a set of runs, not an index by tag, so a commit's `EvictAsync` passes over every run in flight once per evicted tag and asks whether the run's tags contain it (review F2). An index by tag needs a per-tag entry added and removed as runs start and end. Removing a tag's entry when its last run ends races with a run that registers on that tag meanwhile, and closing the race needs a lock or a retry loop, which the brief rules out; the set needs neither. The pass is bounded by the runs in flight on this Host, its concurrent cache misses, never by the tags ever evicted.

The same harness measured `EvictAsync` of one tag with 0, 10, 100 and 1,000 runs in flight on other tags. Each run is a miss whose handler waits on a task the harness completes afterwards. Each shape ran 21 rounds of 10,000 evictions after a warm-up of 1,000, timed and weighed as above, and the record's size, read through reflection, equalled the runs in flight. The build of the tip and the build of the change ran alternately, three times each, on 2026-10-10 (load average about 2).

| Runs in flight on other tags | Tip `b644fe9`, per commit | With the change, per commit |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | 130 to 133 ns, 112 bytes | 262 to 278 ns, 216 bytes |
| 10 | 131 to 136 ns, 112 bytes | 717 to 749 ns, 216 bytes |
| 100 | 133 to 145 ns, 112 bytes | 1,329 to 1,381 ns, 216 bytes |
| 1,000 | 131 to 138 ns, 112 bytes | 13,970 to 19,332 ns, 216 bytes |

The tip's eviction is `RemoveByTagAsync` alone. The change adds about 11 to 19 ns per run in flight, at 100 and 1,000 runs, for each evicted tag, and 104 bytes per commit of one tag, its two enumerators, whatever the number of runs. The pass's first shape matched each run's tags with `Any(tags.Contains)` and allocated a delegate and an enumerator per run: 1,136 bytes per commit with 10 runs, 9,712 with 100 and 96,112 with 1,000, and about 30 to 42 ns per run at 100 and 1,000 runs. Iterating the evicted tags outside the runs, and asking each run's tags `Contains`, removed that allocation. For scale, the commit before it is a PostgreSQL transaction of milliseconds. The per-read numbers above were measured before this change, which touched only `EvictAsync`, a method no read calls.

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since b644fe9` on the ticket's commit `18a22b5`, before this paragraph was added, printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.52`, the version its manifest records for that commit, with the Release runtime suite at 882 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. It ran in full: 4 of the 12 paths changed since `b644fe9` can reach a package, `src/PouryaKit.Caching/HybridQueryCache.cs` among them.

After the dual review, no high finding and five accepted (F1 to F3 medium, F4 and F5 low: the wildcard, the per-commit cost, the probe's disposal, the skill's wildcard sentence and the other-tag test, each fixed above), the same command on the fix round's commit `3f15c69`, before this paragraph was added, printed the same PASS line at version `0.1.0-preview.0.55`, the version its manifest records for that commit, with the Release runtime suite at 885 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. It ran in full: 6 of the 15 paths changed since `b644fe9` can reach a package.

## Pre-Wave 3 a recurring command's cadence after an occurrence that no longer reads, ticket #94

Ticket #94 of spec #74, after T2 of [#82's section](#pre-wave-3-proofs-before-fixes-ticket-82), under the owner's decision of 2026-10-10: find out whether a recurring command can recover safely without unnecessary complexity, build only a proven, bounded solution, and otherwise document the limitation and its operational recovery. The gate of item 2, a check at start of each waiting occurrence's body, is a no-go on its first attempt, at its first condition, so the ticket closes with item 1: the recovery procedure and the shape rule, documented. No Kit code changed but one XML summary, and no public member, package or version pin changed. The ticket also carries the fix of the T2 test's `53300` failure in the per-project Debug run, which [#95's section](#pre-wave-3-an-empty-retry-schedule-refused-at-start-ticket-95) recorded. Environment of every run: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.401, Docker 29.8.1, `postgres:18.6` through Testcontainers, WolverineFx 6.48.3.

| What | Where, or proven by | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Item 1, the recovery procedure | The summary of `AddPouryaKitRecurringCommand` (`src/PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine/DelayedCommands/DelayedCommandServiceCollectionExtensions.cs`); the Kit's skill, `messaging.md`, "Recurring commands", the bullets on a failure and on an occurrence that no longer reads; [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md), the recurring commands bullet | Three steps, each read from the code or shown by #82's run. The signs: the Error entry "Envelope … to local://{name}/ was moved to the error queue" with a `JsonException`, and a dead letter received at that queue, as #82's T2 row quotes. Then start any one instance of the Host: moving the occurrence to the dead letters deleted its `Scheduled` row, so the start stores the first occurrence after now, as the last start of `RecurringCommandTests.An_occurrence_stored_before_its_command_changed_shape_is_set_aside_and_logged_and_its_cadence_waits_for_the_next_host_start` shows. Then discard the dead letter, with `IDeadLetters.DiscardAsync` filtered by its queue and exception type or by deleting its row, and never replay it, since a replay is read as strictly ([ADR 0006](adr/0006-independent-delivery-through-a-routed-queue-per-subscription.md), amendment for #60). Sending the command is no way back: Feature code never schedules a recurring command |
| Item 1, the shape rule | The same three places | Supported: adding members with a default value, and declaring the new shape under a new name, since a starting Host does not count an occurrence waiting on the retired name's queue (`RecurringCommandStart` compares the destination) and that occurrence is set aside when it comes due. Not supported: keeping the old type and forwarding it to the new with Wolverine's `IForwardsTo<T>`. In WolverineFx 6.48.3, decompiled, `HandlerGraph.AddForwarders` builds a forwarding handler only toward a type with a handler chain Wolverine discovered, while `AddMessageHandler` registers a pre-built handler outside the chains; the Kit registers `DelayedCommandDelivery` that way, with conventional discovery off, so no forwarder is built toward it. `RecurringCommandStart` also looks for the declared type's message name, so an occurrence of the old type does not count as waiting. Read from the code, not run. *Corrected on 2026-10-10 by [ticket #90](#pre-wave-3-capability-docs-adr-0006-and-repository-hygiene-ticket-90): the review fix round of this ticket added to the skill's sentence that an occurrence of a type no declaration names is marked handled with no dead letter. That reading stopped at Wolverine's continuation for a type with no handler and missed the missing-message handler (`IMissingHandler`) the Kit's `UnknownMessageBehavior.DeadLetterQueue` registers, which runs first and moves the occurrence to the dead-letter storage, so it is set aside at once, without retries; the skill now says so.* |
| The wording | A search for the phrase the ticket names, the cadence that keeps going, over `src/`, the Kit's skill, `docs/architecture/` and this file, outside `bin/` and `obj/` | Four lines. The summary's continues with its limit, "except one whose stored body no longer reads"; the skill's failure bullet now continues with "unless the occurrence no longer reads"; this file's two are records of #82, its T2 row and its review note. The architecture bullet said, with no limit, that a dead-lettered occurrence leaves the next one stored; it now says so of an occurrence its delivery dead-letters, and states the limit |

| Gate condition of item 2 | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| The waiting rows are read through a store API filtered to the declared queue's `Scheduled` rows, never the whole incoming table at each start and never SQL against Wolverine's table from Kit code | **Fails, on the first attempt**: WolverineFx 6.48.3 has no such API | Every public member of its core, RDBMS and PostgreSQL store code that returns envelopes, decompiled. The scheduled-message query of `IMessageStore.ScheduledMessages` filters `Scheduled` rows by message type, identity and time, and selects the body only to read the tenant from it: its summaries carry no body. `IMessageStore.LoadPageOfGloballyOwnedIncomingAsync`, the one loader filtered by queue, reads only `Incoming` rows owned by no node. The admin's `AllIncomingAsync` reads the whole table. The RDBMS store's `WriteLoadScheduledEnvelopeSql` only writes SQL, over every queue, for its caller to run. Nothing was built around it, as the brief directs |
| The check reuses the one wire reader | Not reached | No body to read without the read above |
| Two Balanced nodes starting at once end with one next occurrence | Not reached | No check, so no test with two Hosts |
| #82's T2 test flips its pinned assertion | Not reached | The test keeps `Assert.Empty(waiting)`: no next occurrence until the next Host start |
| `StrictWireReadingTests` and ADR 0006's strict rule stay untouched | Holds | No reader added. `StrictWireReadingTests` 4 passed, its file unchanged; ADR 0006 unchanged |
| The start's cost is measured and bounded by the declared recurring commands | Not reached | The start is unchanged: one scheduled-message query per declared recurring command |

| What | Proven by | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| The T2 test's `53300` in the per-project Debug run | `RecurringCommandTests.An_occurrence_stored_before_its_command_changed_shape_is_set_aside_and_logged_and_its_cadence_waits_for_the_next_host_start`, its assertions unchanged | The cause: the test composed its three Hosts up front and disposed them only when it ended, so their connection pools stayed open together on the assembly's one PostgreSQL, at its default limit of 100 connections, beside the idle ones earlier tests leave. Each Host is now composed, started, stopped and disposed in a step of its own. Sampling `pg_stat_activity` every 0.25 s while the test ran alone, its database peaked at 75 connections and the server at 79 before the change, at 34 and 38 after. `dotnet test --project tests/ModularMonolith.Tests/ModularMonolith.Tests.csproj` on `ceabe9d`: 608 passed, 0 failed, in 2m29s. `HostFixture`'s connection limit is unchanged, the owner's call |
| The Kit builds and the tests pass | | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors. `RecurringCommandTests` 11 passed; `StrictWireReadingTests` 4 passed; `ModularMonolith.Tests` 608 passed, 0 failed; `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 104 passed. `check-docs.py` and `check-ai-setup.py`: 0 failures |
| For the owner, not fixed here | | ADR 0006's #60 amendment, "Where the failure lands", says `MessageContext.MoveToDeadLetterQueueAsync` finds no native dead-letter queue (`DisableDeadLetterQueueing()`) and calls `IMessageInbox.MoveToDeadLetterStorageAsync`. That holds at the RabbitMQ Host queue, the only place the Kit disables it; on the Kit's durable local queues WolverineFx 6.48.3 takes the native branch of its durable receiver, which calls the same method and writes the same row, so the difference matters only to a dead-letter interceptor, which that branch never runs. *Refined on 2026-10-10 by [ticket #90](#pre-wave-3-capability-docs-adr-0006-and-repository-hygiene-ticket-90): the RabbitMQ Host queue is a durable listener too, which hands the pipeline its durable receiver, so it takes the same native branch; `DisableDeadLetterQueueing()` acts there only by leaving the queue no broker dead-letter sender. No Kit listener runs a dead-letter interceptor (ADR 0006, the dated note in the #60 amendment).* |

## Pre-Wave 3 an empty retry schedule refused at start, ticket #95

Ticket #95 of spec #74, the owner's answer of 2026-10-10 to the question #83's deviation row left open ([its section](#pre-wave-3-option-validation-at-start-ticket-83)): an empty `PouryaKitMessagingOptions.RetryDelays` is refused by the Kit's own validation at start, so every refused value of the option fails the same way, naming it. An intentional change of the failure, not of what is valid: a Host with an empty schedule failed at start before and still does, now with an `OptionsValidationException` naming the option instead of Wolverine's `InvalidOperationException`, "You must specify at least one delay time", which names no Kit setting; a zero delay stays valid. `PouryaKitWolverineExtension` is unchanged, so Wolverine's refusal stays as a backstop a validated Host never reaches. No public member, package or version pin changed. #83's deviation row stays as its record; only its scenario row's test pointer follows the renamed theory. The check is a third `Validate` in `UsePouryaKitWolverine`'s chain, after the null check and before the negative one, and lets null through, so a null schedule still fails with its own message alone | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_null_empty_or_negative_retry_schedule_stops_the_host_at_start_naming_the_option`, four cases: null, empty and #83's two negative ones | With the empty check removed, the empty case alone went red: `Assert.Throws() Failure: Exception type was not an exact match`, expected `OptionsValidationException`, actual `InvalidOperationException`, "You must specify at least one delay time"; the other three cases and the zero-delay test stayed green |
| A zero-delay schedule stays valid | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_zero_delay_retry_schedule_lets_the_host_start`, unchanged | Green with and without the new check |
| The Kit builds and the tests pass | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors. `MisconfiguredHostTests` 29 passed; `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 102 passed. `ModularMonolith.Tests` 600 passed, 1 failed, in each of five runs, four before and one after the rebase onto `a73c2e1`: `RecurringCommandTests.An_occurrence_stored_before_its_command_changed_shape_is_set_aside_and_logged_and_its_cadence_waits_for_the_next_host_start`, on PostgreSQL's `53300: sorry, too many clients already` while its Hosts migrate the message store, the fourth run with no other suite's container on the machine; `RecurringCommandTests` alone passed 11 of 11. On the base `c3a3467`, checked out in the same worktree, the same command failed the same test the same way (599 passed, 1 failed), so the failure is the integration branch's, not this ticket's. The Release runtime suite of the validation below passed it | |

## Pre-Wave 3 the OpenAPI options validated at start, ticket #97

Ticket #97 of spec #74, the fifth option type its problem statement counts: `PouryaKitOpenApiOptions` is validated when the Host starts. An intentional behaviour change: a Host whose sunsets hold one of the four refused values no longer starts, and the `OptionsValidationException` names each refused key. Before #97 a key that names no version threw an `InvalidOperationException` only when Asp.Versioning first read its options, as endpoints were built, at the first request or never, and the other three values were accepted without a word: two keys of one version merged into one policy, the last date winning and both links kept; a relative link was written with a leading `/`; an entry with neither value made an empty policy. No public member, package or version pin changed. `AddSunsets` is unchanged, so its throw stays as a backstop a validated Host never reaches; its parse and the validation's, both through `ApiVersionParser.Default`, are the one duplication the ticket accepts. The shape is a private nested `IValidateOptions<PouryaKitOpenApiOptions>`, registered with `TryAddEnumerable` beside `AddOptions<T>().ValidateOnStart()` as `ConfiguredCors` validates `PouryaKitCorsOptions`, in place of the ticket's literal "shape #83 used", a chain of `Validate(predicate, message)`: a chained message is fixed at registration and cannot name the key, and a sunset dictionary holds several entries (the session's decision of 2026-10-10). Environment of every run: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.401, Docker 29.8.1, `postgres:18.6` through Testcontainers, WolverineFx 6.48.3.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| A key that names no API version (`v1`), two keys that parse to one version (`2` and `2.0`), a `Link` that is not an absolute URI (`/api-v3-sunset`, which the binder reads as a relative `Uri`) and a sunset with neither a `Date` nor a `Link` (`4.0` with no value) each stop the Host at `StartAsync` with an `OptionsValidationException` of `PouryaKitOpenApiOptions`, whose failure line names `PouryaKitOpenApiOptions.Sunsets`, the key, the rule and the fix; two refused entries give a line each. The values are bound from configuration beside the sample's valid `1.0` sunset, as the Hosts bind them, and observed at start, by `ValidateOnStart()`: the test's Host maps no versioned endpoint, so nothing else reads them | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_sunset_naming_no_version_or_a_version_twice_or_a_relative_link_or_nothing_stops_the_host_at_start_naming_its_key`, five cases | With the key check removed, the `v1` case and the two-entry case went red; with the duplicate check removed, the `2` and `2.0` case; with the absolute-URI check removed, the relative-link case and the two-entry case; with the empty-entry check removed, the `4.0` case; with only `ValidateOnStart()` removed, all five, whose Hosts then started. With the duplicates grouped by the raw key instead of the parsed version, the `2` and `2.0` case went red |
| The sample's own sunset, bound from its `appsettings.json` in the tests' output, and no sunset at all let the Host start, and the bound keys are `1.0` and none | `MisconfiguredHostTests.The_samples_sunset_or_none_lets_the_host_start`, two cases | Green under each of the six variants above |
| The sample's Host still reports its configured sunset | `OpenApiTests`, the whole class | |
| Refuted: the ticket's item 1 says `ValidateOnStart()` also makes a malformed `Date` fail at start, in the binder, naming the section, an inference of the scout's that no run had checked. In a Host, `Configure<T>(section)` uses the reflection binder, whose `BindDictionary` (`Microsoft.Extensions.Configuration.Binder` of the shared framework 10.0.12, decompiled) catches any exception while binding one entry and rethrows only when `BinderOptions.ErrorOnUnknownConfiguration` is set. So `PouryaKit:OpenApi:Sunsets:5.0:Date` set to `next June`, or a `Link` of `http://`, drops the whole `5.0` entry without an error: the version reports no sunset, and the validation never sees the entry. A first probe, a file-based app (`dotnet run probe.cs`), threw an `InvalidOperationException` naming the path, because such an app enables the source-generated binder, which throws; the same probe with `PublishAot` and `EnableConfigurationBindingGenerator` off dropped the entry, and threw with `ErrorOnUnknownConfiguration` set | A Host of the theory's shape, with the sample's configuration and `5.0:Date` set to `next June`, started, and its bound `Sunsets` held `1.0` alone; the fact planned for the binder's failure was dropped. The option's summary and the skill's `openapi.md` state the silent drop | Open for the owner: `ErrorOnUnknownConfiguration` where a Host binds is a composition policy over every option bound from configuration, not a sunset check; the session decided on 2026-10-10 that this ticket changes no Host |
| The Kit builds and the tests pass | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors. `MisconfiguredHostTests` 36 passed; `OpenApiTests` 14 passed; `ModularMonolith.Tests` 608 passed, 0 failed, in each of two runs, the known `53300` failure of [#95's section](#pre-wave-3-an-empty-retry-schedule-refused-at-start-ticket-95) occurring in neither; `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 102 passed | |
| Docs | The XML summaries of `PouryaKitOpenApiOptions.Sunsets` and `AddPouryaKitOpenApi`; the Kit's skill, `openapi.md` ("Deprecation and sunset", "Testing versions and documents"); [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md), a bullet in `PouryaKit.Web.OpenApi`. They say what is refused and that the Host does not start, and what stays accepted: a past date, since nothing reads the clock, and a version no Module declares, known only once the endpoints are mapped. A search for the skill's old sentence on a key that names no version, across `src/`, the skill, `docs/architecture/` and this file, outside `bin/` and `obj/`, prints nothing | |

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since a04d2a9` on the ticket's commit `6830434`, before this paragraph was added, printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.49`, the version its manifest records for that commit, with the Release runtime suite at 879 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. It ran in full: 3 of the 8 paths changed since `a04d2a9` can reach a package, `src/PouryaKit.Web.OpenApi/PouryaKitOpenApiOptions.cs` among them. The sample's Host and both generated Hosts, whose sunset is the valid `1.0` one, still start.

## Final quality pass: Core, Domain, Cqrs, Validation and Modularity, ticket #99

Ticket #99 of the final quality pass before the push ([#98](https://github.com/PouryaNoufallah96/PouryaKit/issues/98)): the six low findings a quality scout confirmed in Wave 1 and 2 code of these packages, each fixed at its source with a focused test, and one correction of this record. No public API change: a reflection dump of the exported types of Core, Domain, Cqrs, Modularity and Validation and of every public and protected member, 85 types in 551 lines, is identical before and after. No package or version pin changed, and `PouryaKit.Validation`'s code is unchanged. Environment of every run: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.401, Docker 29.8.1, `postgres:18.6` through Testcontainers, Microsoft.Extensions.* 10.0.12, xunit.v3 4.0.1.

| Item | The fix, and proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| 1. A keyed service of a handler's type | `AddHandler` matched any descriptor of the handler's service type. A correction to the ticket, which says `ImplementationType` throws on a keyed descriptor: on Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection.Abstractions 10.0.12 it returns null, as a probe showed, so the old code read the keyed service as a second handler and stopped registration with a false "has 2 handlers". It now skips keyed descriptors, as Modularity's ownership record does. `RegistrationTests.A_keyed_service_of_a_handlers_type_is_no_handler_and_the_scanned_handler_is_registered_beside_it`: the keyed registration stays, and the scanned handler is registered unkeyed beside it | With the keyed filter removed: `InvalidOperationException`, "PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests.Module.GuardedByCommandCommand has 2 handlers; exactly one is required." |
| 2. The Gateway's usage errors | The missing-context check and the ownership check ran inside the async core, so they surfaced as a faulted task, where a missing command threw at once. They now run in a method that is not async, which then starts the scoped run, a private async method, so every usage error throws before the caller gets a task and a call allocates no closure for the run. `GatewayTests.A_missing_context_or_an_operation_the_module_does_not_own_throws_before_the_gateway_returns_a_task`: each of the three methods with no context throws `ArgumentNullException` naming `context`; Catalog's command, command with a response and query, and a command no Module handles, sent through the Stub Module's Gateway, each throw `InvalidOperationException`; every case is asserted with `Assert.Throws` on the call itself | With the old Gateway processor: "No exception was thrown", 3 of 3 cases without a context; with that half of the test removed, 4 of 4 operations the Module does not own |
| 3. The failure log's level | The dispatcher joined a failed Operation's error codes before the generated method checked the level. It now calls `LogFailed` only when `logger.IsEnabled(LogLevel.Information)`, and the generated method skips its own check (`SkipEnabledCheck`). `FailureLogTests.A_failed_operation_joins_its_error_codes_only_when_its_log_entry_is_written`: a query fails with two codes of 20,000 characters each, and a logger whose entries switch on and off writes nothing; with entries off the synchronous dispatch allocates less than the 80,004 bytes of the joined codes, with them on at least that | With the old dispatcher, entries off: 82,224 bytes |
| 4. The typed-ID factory | `TypedId.From` called the constructor through `ConstructorInvoker.Invoke(object)`, which boxed the value of every ID the EF Core converter and the Dapper handler read. It now calls a lambda compiled once per ID type from `Expression.New`, which passes the constructor's own exceptions through unwrapped, as the invoker did: `TypedIdTests.Empty_values_are_rejected` and `TypedIdTests.An_empty_id_in_json_is_a_json_error` pass unchanged. `TypedIdTests.An_id_created_from_its_value_allocates_no_more_than_its_constructor`: after one warm-up call, 1,000 calls of `From` allocate what 1,000 `new` calls do | With the old factory: expected 32,000 bytes, actual 64,000 |
| 5. `DispatchTests`' predicate | `DispatchTests.A_unit_of_work_that_reports_a_success_other_than_the_commands_result_throws` asserted only that the message names `IUnitOfWork`, which two other dispatcher messages also do; it now asserts the sentence "returned a success that is not the command's own Result" | With the dispatcher throwing its rollback-only message at that point instead, the new assertion failed ("Sub-string not found") where the old one passed |
| 6. `ClockTests`' rendezvous | The two Operations of `ClockTests.A_domain_event_raised_in_an_operation_takes_its_hosts_clock_and_parallel_operations_keep_their_own` wait for each other with no cancellation, so one that fails leaves the other waiting forever. The test now has a 30-second timeout, reads `TestContext.Current.CancellationToken` and passes it to both Operations, and the handler waits with `WaitAsync` on the token it receives | With the second Operation replaced by a failed task: the old test was still running after 120 seconds and was killed; the new one failed at 30 seconds with an `AggregateException` holding "The tuesday Operation failed." and xUnit's timeout |
| 7. This record's lookup rule | The rule row on implementing-type lookup, near the top of this file, and the `TypeLookupTests` row of [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md) said every Kit package but Core asks `ImplementingTypes`; both now name the exception, `PouryaKit.Validation`, which finds its validators with FluentValidation's own assembly scanner on purpose (its [provenance record](provenance/PouryaKit.Validation.md)) | |
| For the owner, not changed here | The scout's reading: FluentValidation's assembly scanner silently skips a generic or an abstract validator, where Cqrs's registration refuses a generic handler loudly | |

The measurement of item 4, once before and once after, a throwaway console project outside the repository referencing the five packages, Release, a million calls of `TypedId.From` for a Guid ID per round, five rounds, allocations read with `GC.GetAllocatedBytesForCurrentThread`:

| | Time per million calls | Allocated per call | First call for an ID type |
|---|---|---|---|
| Before, `ConstructorInvoker` | 13.9 to 14.6 ms | 64 bytes | 1.1 ms for the first type, 31 µs for the next |
| After, the compiled lambda | 6.2 to 6.8 ms | 32 bytes | 7.9 ms for the first type, 148 µs for the next |
| `new` of the same ID, both runs | 6.0 to 6.6 ms | 32 bytes | |

The compiled lambda is faster and allocates half, the same as the constructor, so it stays. The same project created an ID whose constructor is private through `From`, and an empty value of it surfaced as `ArgumentException`. Its one-time cost is higher: the first ID type loads the expression compiler, and each later type compiles its own lambda.

| What | Evidence |
|---|---|
| The Kit builds and the tests pass | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors. `PouryaKit.Core.Tests` 17 passed; `PouryaKit.Domain.Tests` 41; `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests` 48; `GatewayTests` 5; `ModularMonolith.Tests` 614 passed, 0 failed, in 2m30s; `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 104. `check-docs.py` and `check-ai-setup.py`: 0 failures |
| Docs | No doc restates the behaviour items 1 to 6 change; the code comments at each fix say what they guard. Item 7 is the docs change. From the docs accuracy review: the summary of `AddPouryaKitCqrs` and four sentences of the Kit's skill, three in `commands-queries-validation.md` and one in `persistence.md`, said a handler, authorizer or Domain Event Handler the registration refuses fails at startup; they now say it fails while the Host is composed, the repository's phrase for a throw inside a registration, since the glossary's Startup check names the later moment. No other summary of these five packages said it |

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since ebde00d` on the ticket's commit `0abd742`, before this paragraph named it, printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.78`, the version its manifest records for that commit, with the Release runtime suite at 890 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. It ran in full: 10 of the 16 paths changed since `ebde00d` can reach a package, `src/PouryaKit.Cqrs/CqrsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs` among them. A first run on `65168dd`, whose Gateway processor ran the scope in an async local function that allocated a closure per call, passed the same way at `0.1.0-preview.0.76`.

## Final quality pass: Persistence and Messaging, ticket #100

Ticket #100 of the final quality pass (#98): the confirmed problems of the Persistence and Messaging quality scout, the selector concern of #92's close-out, and a live test of the dead-lettering of a stored type no declaration names, fixed at the source with focused tests. No public member, exported type, package or version pin changed: every changed declaration is private or internal. Items 1, 2, 3 and 5 each change a behaviour on purpose, as their rows say. Environment of every run: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.401, Docker 29.8.1, `postgres:18.6` through Testcontainers, WolverineFx 6.48.3, EF Core 10.0.12.

| Item and behaviour | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| 1. When the attempt that sets a recurring occurrence aside fails to store the next occurrence too, it rethrows its own failure, which the dead letter records, and logs an Error whose `AggregateException` holds the store's failure, then the attempt's. Nothing then waits until a replay of the dead letter or a Host start stores the first occurrence after now, the recovery #94 documented | `RecurringCommandTests.An_occurrence_set_aside_while_storing_the_next_occurrence_fails_keeps_its_own_failure_and_logs_the_store_failure`: a Stub recurring command at a one-second cadence whose occurrence factory throws once the Host has started, every delivery failed after its Receipt | With the store called outside a try of its own, as before: the dead letter recorded `System.TimeoutException`, "The test failed to store an occurrence.", the factory's, instead of the delivery's `InvalidOperationException` |
| 2. `EfReceipts` refuses a Receipt and a Snapshot version where the Unit of Work has no transaction, "No Unit of Work is active, so there is no transaction to keep Receipts in." and its version counterpart, as `EfIdempotencyStore` refuses a key, and writes nothing | `UnitOfWorkTests.A_receipt_or_a_snapshot_version_recorded_where_no_unit_of_work_is_active_is_refused_and_nothing_is_kept`, in a scope begun in the Stub's Module that runs no command | With the guard off, both rows committed on their own: `[[1, 1]]` where `[[0, 0]]` was expected |
| 3. Dapper reads named `app` and then `APP` share one connection, compared ignoring case as configuration keys and `AddPouryaKitEfCore` compare them | `DapperTests.Dapper_reads_named_again_in_another_case_share_the_one_connection` | With the ordinal comparison: "Dapper reads already use the connection 'app' in this Host, so they cannot also use 'APP'." |
| 4. A scope resolves one `WolverineOutbox` as both `IUnitOfWorkOutbox` and `IOutbox`: it is registered once and both forward to it, as `PublishedIntegrationEvents` and `ScheduledDelayedCommands` are. `CommitProbe` now decorates through the forwarding factory | `UnitOfWorkTests.A_scope_resolves_one_outbox_for_its_unit_of_work_and_the_hosts_outbox_marker`, on the sample's Host as it composes itself | With the two type registrations back, and `CommitProbe` back on the implementation type: `Assert.Same() Failure: Values are not the same instance` |
| 5. `PageSql`'s constructor refuses, naming the field, a declared filter whose value type its own table of database types lacks, so a value type `FilterField` gains alone fails when the Feature's SQL is built, not at the first request that filters on it | `PagingTests.A_page_sql_whose_filter_has_a_value_type_it_has_no_database_type_for_is_refused_when_built`, over a `FilterField` of `DateOnly` made without its factory, since `FilterField.Of` refuses every type outside its list | With the check off: `Assert.Throws() Failure: No exception was thrown` |
| 6. `SnapshotStep` caches per model only what the model decides: each aggregate's root and the properties in its graph whose values the database may generate. Whether a client-side value generator sets one for good is asked of the committing DbContext's `IValueGeneratorSelector` at each commit, so two contexts given one model with different selectors each get their own answer, in either order | `SnapshotTests.A_snapshot_is_refused_by_the_value_generator_selector_its_dbcontext_uses_when_two_share_a_model`, without a database: one context keeps Npgsql's selector, the other replaces it with one that returns a generator of temporary values whatever the mapping names; both expose one model instance | With the previous `SnapshotStep`: the second context's commit was not refused, `Assert.Throws() Failure: No exception was thrown` |
| 7. A delayed command stored by one Host and found due by another, on the same new database, that declares nothing for its type is dead-lettered the first time it is handed out: one dead letter, with its id, `local://stub.retired-type/`, `Wolverine.Runtime.Interop.UnknownMessageTypeNameException` and "Unknown message type: 'ModularMonolith.Tests.Stub.WriteKindChangedNoteCommand'"; its envelope's attempts 0, no incoming row, no note and no delivery attempt of the Kit. Only the final state is asserted, since the move to the dead letters and the mark as handled are two queued writes in either order. Each Host is composed, started, stopped and disposed in a step of its own | `DelayedCommandTests.A_delayed_command_whose_type_no_declaration_names_any_more_is_dead_lettered_at_once_and_never_run` | With `RemoveAll<IMissingHandler>()` in the second Host's compose hook: no dead letter within 15 s. The lever works because Wolverine runs `UseWolverine`'s options lambda at registration, where the `UnknownMessageBehavior` setter adds that handler to the Host's services |

Item 6 was measured before the change, as the ticket asks: `SnapshotStep.Build` called by reflection on the fixture Host's `StubDbContext`, with a tracked badge, the Stub aggregate whose code `HasValueGenerator<GuidValueGenerator>` sets, so the selector decides it, and the Host's Snapshot mappers. Each run built 2,000 times to warm up, then timed seven rounds of 1,000 builds; a throwaway probe, never committed. ## Final quality pass: Web, OpenAPI, Security, Observability, Caching and Testing, ticket #101

Ticket #101 of the final quality pass before the push (#98): the seven confirmed problems of the Web, Security, Observability, Caching and Testing quality scout, fixed at the source. One public API change, item 6: `PouryaKitOpenApiOptions.Sunsets` is declared `IDictionary<string, ApiVersionSunset>`, where it was the concrete `Dictionary<,>`, backed by an ordinal `Dictionary` as before. No other public member, exported type name, package or version pin changed. Two registrations now refuse or ignore what they accepted: a second `AddPouryaKitEndpoints` for a declared route prefix or assembly throws, and a second `AddPouryaKitOpenApi` adds only its `configure`. Environment of every run: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.401, Docker 29.8.1, `postgres:18.6` through Testcontainers, Asp.Versioning 10.2.3, OpenTelemetry 1.19.1.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| Item 1. `AddPouryaKitEndpoints` refuses a route prefix already declared, and an assembly whose endpoints are already declared under another prefix, as `AddPouryaKitApiVersions` refuses a prefix twice: either would map the endpoints twice, and each request to one of them would fail as ambiguous. The prefix is checked before any assembly is scanned. The guard reads every Module's declarations, since a Module's builder enumerates the Host's services | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_route_group_or_an_assembly_whose_endpoints_are_already_declared_is_refused_when_declared_again`, two cases: `/plain` again with another assembly, and the same assembly under `/other` | With the prefix check off, the `/plain` case went red; with the assembly check off, the `/other` case: `Assert.Throws() Failure: No exception was thrown` |
| Item 2. `HostFixture.DisposeAsync` stops the Host, then disposes it in a `finally`, through `IAsyncDisposable` where the Host offers it, as a `WebApplication` does, and else `Dispose`. It disposes the database container in an outer `finally`, whatever the Host's stop or disposal did; a failure still reaches the caller. `InitializeAsync` passes `TestContext.Current.CancellationToken` to the container's start and the Host's start | `HostFixtureTests.A_host_that_fails_to_stop_is_still_disposed_asynchronously_and_the_database_container_with_it`: a derived fixture whose Host, a test double with no services, fails to stop. Its real container starts; `DisposeAsync` throws the stop's exception; the double records one `DisposeAsync` and no `Dispose`; and a connection without pooling to the captured connection string then fails with an `NpgsqlException` | With the old body, red: the double recorded no disposal. With `Dispose` in place of `DisposeAsync`, red: it recorded `Dispose`. With the container disposed after the outer `try` instead of in its `finally`, red: the connection opened |
| Item 3. With the rate limit on, `HttpCallerContext.Resolve` builds the request's Operation Context once: the limiter's partition resolves it, keeps it among the request's features, and the endpoint's Operation begins with that same context, one `OperationId`. It is reused only while the request's endpoint and caller are the ones it was resolved from: a limiter placed before routing saw no endpoint, and authorization placed after the limiter may replace the caller. A resolution that throws is not kept, so a trusted peer's unreadable headers still answer 400 from the endpoint | `WebDefaultsTests.With_the_rate_limit_on_the_limiter_and_the_endpoint_share_one_resolution_of_the_callers_context`: an `IClaimsTransformation` hands out the caller as a principal that counts the reads of its Actor claim; one `/stub/context` request as ada reads it once. `WebDefaultsTests`, the whole class, unchanged, including the unreadable-headers case | With reuse off, red: `("ada", 2)` instead of `("ada", 1)` |
| Item 4. The duration histogram's `Meter` comes from the Host container's `IMeterFactory`, through one internal singleton per container that the dispatcher's factory resolves, so each Host owns its instruments and disposes them with itself; the `ActivitySource` stays static. Every Host has an `IMeterFactory`: `HostBuilder.PopulateServiceCollection` calls `AddMetrics` (Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting 10.0.12, decompiled), and `AddMetrics` itself lives in `Microsoft.Extensions.Diagnostics`, outside Observability's closure, so `AddPouryaKitObservability` does not call it. OpenTelemetry 1.19.1's meter provider selects meters by name only (`MeterProviderSdk`, decompiled), so an exporter still sees every Host of a process | `ObservabilityTests.A_commands_duration_is_measured_on_a_meter_of_the_hosts_own_meter_factory`: each duration a `WriteNoteCommand` records comes from a meter whose `Scope` is the fixture Host's `IMeterFactory`. The other `ObservabilityTests`, which listen by meter name, and `HostRecipeTests`, which export through OpenTelemetry, pass unchanged | With a static `new Meter(...)` in its place, red: `Assert.Same() Failure`, the scope was null |
| Item 5. `StoredDeliveries.DeleteAllAsync` runs its three `DELETE` statements with `ExecuteNonQueryAsync`, not through a reader | Every `HostTest` resets through it before each test: `ModularMonolith.Tests`, the whole suite | |
| Item 6. `Sunsets` is an `IDictionary` with ordinal keys. Asp.Versioning ignores the case of a version's status (`ApiVersion` hashes `Status` with `StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase`; Asp.Versioning.Abstractions 10.2.1, decompiled), so `1.0-Beta` and `1.0-beta` name one version: the dictionary keeps both keys, and #97's validation refuses them by name, where a case-insensitive one would keep the last without a word. The binder still binds the interface, the sample's sunset included | `MisconfiguredHostTests.Two_sunset_keys_that_differ_only_in_case_stop_the_host_at_start_naming_both`. The binding: `MisconfiguredHostTests.The_samples_sunset_or_none_lets_the_host_start` and the sunset theory of #97, unchanged | With `StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase`, red: the Host started |
| Item 7. Verified: Asp.Versioning.OpenApi 10.2.3's `AddOpenApi(configure)` calls `services.Configure<VersionedOpenApiOptions>(configure)` on every call (decompiled), so a second `AddPouryaKitOpenApi` ran each operation transformer twice, and an operation listed its `Idempotency-Key` parameter twice; the sunset `Link` header stayed single. Fixed with the siblings' idiom: a second call adds only its `configure` and returns, found by the sunset validation it registered | `OpenApiTests.A_second_registration_of_the_api_versions_and_documents_adds_nothing`: the sample's Host with one more `AddPouryaKitOpenApi()` lists the header once on each operation that requires it | Without the guard, red: "Sequence contains more than one element" on the duplicated parameter. With the `Link` header asserted first, the header passed, so the test does not assert it |
| The one file pair outside the ticket's list, granted by the session on 2026-10-10: `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests`' allocation test builds a bare `ServiceCollection`, which has no `IMeterFactory`, and failed with "Unable to resolve service for type 'System.Diagnostics.Metrics.IMeterFactory'". Its container now calls `AddMetrics()`, through a test-only reference to `Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting` (pinned 10.0.12, as `PouryaKit.Security.Tests` references it); its assertions are unchanged | `ObservedDispatcherTests.With_nothing_listening_the_observed_dispatcher_returns_the_dispatchers_own_task_and_allocates_nothing`, still allocation-free; `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests` 46 passed | |
| The Kit builds and the tests pass | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors. `WebDefaultsTests` 18 passed; `ObservabilityTests` 8 passed; `ModularMonolith.Tests` 620 passed, 0 failed; `PouryaKit.Security.Tests` 61 passed; `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 104 passed; `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests` 46 passed | |
| Docs | The XML summaries of `AddPouryaKitEndpoints`, `AddPouryaKitObservability`, `AddPouryaKitOpenApi`, `PouryaKitOpenApiOptions.Sunsets` and `HostFixture.DisposeAsync`; the Kit's skill, `web.md` (the endpoints' declaration, the rate limit), `openapi.md` (the registration, the sunsets) and `observability.md`, which says why every Host has an `IMeterFactory`; [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md), the paragraphs of the endpoints' declaration, the rate limit, `AddPouryaKitObservability`, `AddPouryaKitOpenApi`, the sunsets and `HostFixture` | |

## Final quality pass before the push, ticket #98

Ticket #98 (a sub-issue of spec #74): the owner's final quality and consistency pass over Waves 1, 2 and the Pre-Wave 3 consolidation, on `pre-wave-3`, before the push. Its four work packages (#99 to #102, direct sub-issues of #74) have their own sections above; this section holds the pass's remaining concerns, dispositions and the final verification of the branch at `427f111`. Environment of every run: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.401, Docker 29.8.1, `postgres:18.6`, `rabbitmq:4.3.6` and `redis:8.10.1` through Testcontainers, WolverineFx 6.48.3.

| Concern left by #92 | Classification and evidence | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| A one-off 0 ms failure of `SnapshotTests.A_snapshot_is_refused_by_the_model_its_dbcontext_uses_when_one_dbcontext_type_has_two_models` | Test infrastructure, most likely a one-off start of the assembly fixture: the test resets the database through `HostFixture.ResetAsync` first, and xUnit v3 reports 0 ms only when the test body never ran because setup already failed or the run was cancelled; all tests run in one sequential collection, each builds fresh models, the cache is thread-safe. Ten runs of the class (34 tests each) and twenty of the single test: all passed | Not reproduced; recorded with its uncertainty (the filter the implementer used and the lost message are unknown). No retry, suppression or weaker test |
| The value-generator selector versus the Snapshot step's model key | Intentional under EF's and Npgsql's shipped selectors (both answer from the property's own factory, a model fact); latent only with a custom selector that ignores the factory and a model shared through `UseModel`, a compiled model or a shared memory cache, which nothing in the Kit, sample or tests does | Fixed anyway in #100 item 6 at no measurable cost: the model-only part cached, the selector asked per commit (`SnapshotTests.A_snapshot_is_refused_by_the_value_generator_selector_its_dbcontext_uses_when_two_share_a_model`, red once) |
| No live test of unknown-message-type dead-lettering | Real gap: ADR 0006 claimed it from decompilation | Pinned in #100 item 7: `DelayedCommandTests.A_delayed_command_whose_type_no_declaration_names_any_more_is_dead_lettered_at_once_and_never_run`, two Hosts over a new database, the final state asserted; red once with `RemoveAll<IMissingHandler>()`; the ADR points at it |
| The three retro candidates | Process defects, judgement rules: a report is a claim about a moment (an implementer may commit after it); a brief names the frontier it was written for; a failure seen twice is not a transient | Three bullets in `docs/agents/running-a-spec.md` "State and handoff" (`ebde00d`). The first applied to this session within the hour: #99's and #100's implementers committed after reporting, the merge script refused the stale heads, and the late commits were cherry-picked after the merges |
| API consistency and behaviour | The #92 scout's facts and the three quality scouts' findings (session-local files beside the session's handoff, not in the repository; their confirmed items are the tickets #99 to #101 and their sections above) | Confirmed problems fixed in #99 to #101; the rest intentional or declined, below |

Deliberately left unchanged, with the reason: `AddPouryaKitJwtBearer` returns `AuthenticationBuilder` and `AddPouryaKitRecurringCommand` an `OptionsBuilder<T>` (each hands the caller the builder it needs next); `IQueryCache.EvictAsync` and `IUnitOfWorkOutbox.FlushAsync` take no `CancellationToken` (both run inside a commit that must finish once started); `HostFixture.DeadLettersAsync`'s noun name; `PredicateBuilder` public though used by tests and the skill only (a Feature author's helper); the five-copy `In(services)` registry idiom (a shared helper would be a new public API in `PouryaKit.Core` for four lines); the Dapper factory's throw beside `ReadConnectionCheck` (the `??` gives the constructor a non-null string); the two option-validation shapes (#83's rule); the eighteen `src/` path citations in `docs/adr/` that #78's moves outdated (dated records, by `check-docs.py`'s design); a shared schema-qualified-name helper and a shared read-only guard (new abstractions); `UsePouryaKitWolverine` called twice throws Wolverine's own exception (now stated in its summary); `params Assembly[]` on the registrations (Kit-wide shape); route prefixes differing only in case still map twice (a Kit-wide normalization decision); the architecture README's per-package "Seams" bullets; FluentValidation's scanner for validators (the library's own facility; the acceptance record names the exception; it skips an abstract class as Cqrs does and also skips an open generic validator where Cqrs refuses a generic handler definition, so only the generic case differs, for the owner); `JwtBearerValidation` not refusing `RequireHttpsMetadata = false` (not a documented guarantee, for the owner); the rate-limit queue test's 3-second window (never seen failing); `TrackedState`'s capture per read-only step (#84's V1 measured about 1 µs per tracked entity per nested query; a decision on that number for Wave 3's deeper nesting, recorded in the plan's I.6, for the owner).

The selector returns `TemporaryLongValueGenerator` for that key and `NpgsqlSequentialGuidValueGenerator`, whose values are not temporary, for a `Guid` child key. `SnapshotTests.A_snapshot_is_refused_when_the_database_generates_the_key_of_a_child_in_its_aggregates_graph` was red on the old guard (`Assert.Throws() Failure: No exception was thrown`). The commit that changed `ci.yml`, which ci-and-publication cites, passed its own pre-commit hook: the hook's check compares the revision with HEAD, which does not yet hold the commit. The next commit's hook refused it, and CI checks the committed tree. The rebase onto `cdaa353` rewrote that commit, and the check reported the old revision as unreachable until ci-and-publication pinned the rewritten one, `24e4921`, whose `ci.yml` is byte-identical |

## Tenant-scoped Modules, ticket #116

Ticket #116 of spec #115 (Wave 3): multi-tenancy as registrations across existing packages, no new package. `ITenantScoped` (`PouryaKit.Domain`) marks an aggregate as one tenant's; `PouryaKitDbContext.TenantFilter` (`PouryaKit.Persistence.EFCore`) reads it only for the Operation's tenant; `AddPouryaKitTenancy` (`PouryaKit.Cqrs`) makes a Module, or an application without Modules, tenant-scoped, and its `Tenant.Required` authorizer refuses an Operation without a tenant, a trusted continuation included. Catalog is tenant-scoped in the Proving Sample. Environment: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.402, Docker 29.8.2, EF Core 10.0.12, Npgsql EF Core 10.0.3. Each test below was run once with the behaviour it names switched off, through a scratchpad script that changed one source, ran the class and restored the file.

| Item | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A tenant-scoped Module's EF Core reads return only the Operation's tenant's rows | Tenant A's rename of B's product is `Product.NotFound`, A's removal by a name both tenants use removes A's product only, and A's get and list show none of B's | `TenancyTests.A_tenant_reads_writes_and_removes_none_of_another_tenants_products_through_the_gateway`. Red with the convention adding no tenant filter, and red with GetProduct's or ListProducts' SQL without `tenant = @Tenant` |
| A read that sets the named filter aside crosses tenants, and no other read does | A read with `IgnoreQueryFilters([PouryaKitDbContext.TenantFilter])` returns both tenants' products and still leaves a removed one out; the same read without it returns the Operation's tenant's only, also from a DbContext resolved before the scope's Operation began; a DbContext in a scope where no Operation began builds and reads no row | `TenancyTests.A_read_that_sets_the_tenant_filter_aside_crosses_tenants_and_no_other_read_does`. Red with no tenant filter; red with the tenant read once, when the model is built (every read empty, since `HostFixture` migrates where no Operation began); red with the tenant read when the DbContext is created |
| Over HTTP, with the test scheme's tenant claim | Tenant A's GET of B's product answers 404, B's own GET answers 200, and A's list holds A's product only | `TenancyTests.Over_http_a_tenant_gets_and_lists_none_of_another_tenants_products`. Red with GetProduct's or ListProducts' SQL without `tenant = @Tenant` |
| An Operation without a tenant fails forbidden before its handler runs | A command, the same command with a blank name its validator would refuse, and a query whose handler would answer `NotFound` each fail with `TenantErrors.Required`; over HTTP the list answers 403 with `Tenant.Required` in `errors`; no category is written | `TenancyTests.An_operation_without_a_tenant_fails_forbidden_before_its_validator_and_handler_run`. Red with the requirement allowing every Operation |

Red with the requirement letting a trusted continuation through: the tenantless question ran, read nothing and answered unknown |

Red with the declaration registering nothing on the Host's own services. `RegistrationTests.A_tenancy_declaration_on_the_hosts_services_that_names_no_assembly_fails` (`PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests`): red without the throw |
| A command declared outside the tenant-scoped assembly (PR #143, Codex P1) | A command of `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests.Contracts`, handled in the test Module's assembly, with both declared through `AddPouryaKitTenancy(module)` and `AddPouryaKitCqrs(module)`: sent with no tenant, it fails with `Tenant.Required` and its handler never runs | `TenancyTests.A_command_declared_in_another_assembly_and_handled_in_a_tenant_scoped_one_needs_a_tenant` (`PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests`). Red with the requirement registered only for the operations the assemblies declare |

## Number sequences, ticket #122

Ticket #122 of spec #115: a gap-free number per scope key inside the Unit of Work, as a registration in `PouryaKit.Persistence.Postgres`. A Module calls `AddPouryaKitNumberSequences()` on its builder and creates `pouryakit_number_sequences` with `CreateNumberSequenceTable(schema)`; a handler asks `INumberSequences.NextAsync(scopeKey)` for a `Result<long>`. The allocation joins the Unit of Work's transaction through `IUnitOfWorkConnection.Join()` and sends one Npgsql batch: an insert of the scope row that does nothing on conflict, then an update that adds one and returns the value. The Proving Sample's Inventory numbers its stock counts per year (`StartStockCount`, migration `StockCounts`). Environment: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.402, Docker 29.8.2, Npgsql 10.0.3, Archify 3.0.1 with Playwright's Chromium headless.

| Item | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Two concurrent Operations on one scope key get consecutive numbers, the second waiting | `NumberSequenceTests.Two_operations_on_one_scope_key_get_consecutive_numbers_and_the_second_waits_for_the_first_to_commit`: the first stock count, begun as an Inventory Operation, takes number 1 and is held before its commit; the second, sent through Inventory's Gateway, is seen waiting on a lock on the sequence table in `pg_stat_activity` and has not returned; once the first commits it receives 2 | Red once with the allocation on a connection of its own outside the transaction: the second never waited (`The second stock count did not wait on the first's number sequence row.` after 15 s), though the numbers were still consecutive |
| A rolled-back Operation leaves no gap | `NumberSequenceTests.A_rolled_back_operation_gives_its_number_back_to_the_next_operation`: an Operation that starts a stock count, then fails with an Error, is rolled back; the next stock count receives the number it released, 1, and only that row is stored | Red once with the same own-connection allocation: `Assert.Equal() Failure: Values differ`, the next number was 2 |
| A cancelled Operation leaves no gap (ticket #146) | `NumberSequenceTests.A_cancelled_operation_gives_its_number_back_to_the_next_operation`: an Operation that starts a stock count, taking number 1, is held before its commit, then its caller cancels; the Operation ends in an `OperationCanceledException` and is rolled back, the next stock count receives 1, and only that row is stored. A transaction whose connection drops before its commit is rolled back by PostgreSQL, as its documentation states; no Kit test shows it, and the boundary comment on `PostgresNumberSequences` and the skill say so | Red once with the allocation on a connection of its own outside the transaction, run alone: `Assert.Equal() Failure: Values differ`, expected 1, actual 2 |
| A deadlock is a conflict | `NumberSequenceTests.Two_operations_that_deadlock_on_two_scope_keys_commit_once_and_the_other_fails_with_a_conflict`: two Operations take numbers of `north` and `south` in opposite orders; PostgreSQL aborts one wait (40P01), which fails with `UnitOfWorkErrors.ConcurrencyConflict` and commits nothing; the other commits both numbers | Red once with the 40P01 mapping off: `Npgsql.PostgresException : 40P01: deadlock detected` reached the caller |
| A Module without the migration is told which helper to call | `NumberSequenceTests.A_module_without_the_table_is_told_to_add_the_migration_that_creates_it`: the test Module registers number sequences without the table; the first number throws `StubDbContext has no "stub".pouryakit_number_sequences table to keep its number sequences in. Add a migration to the Module that calls CreateNumberSequenceTable with its schema.` | Red once with the 42P01 catch off: `Assert.Throws() Failure: Exception type was not an exact match` (the `PostgresException` itself) |
| The skill and the plan | [persistence.md](../.agents/skills/pourya-kit/references/persistence.md#number-sequences) states that Operations on one scope key run one at a time until commit; the I.3 row of [the plan](foundation-review.md) carries a dated "Built" note recording that its `Conflict` question dissolved: the allocator raises no unique violation, and a unique violation on the business column that holds the number is the existing EF-save mapping to the Module's Error | |
| Diagrams | `kit-context` and `proving-sample-and-templates` cite `InventoryModule.cs`, which gained the registration: the first range (line 21) is unchanged, the second moved its end from line 35 to 37 with the same label; both pin `c21ed9f`, the commit that changed the file. kit-context refinalized: `ok true, status pass, gates validate pass, deliver pass, check pass, browser-check pass, diagnostics 0`; proving-sample-and-templates: as above | kit-context source SHA-256 `8417861ac39391f25c64657a2c6c41e0d00ccff9e934e2c93891782f6608697b`, HTML `4cdfedb91c57dc5fbaada67af7b950d6b8e6c6a680f5ad207eee63e737426b70`; proving-sample-and-templates source `af25d1e21e4665f65c08912d84139636145b9242e647d67bff630ba469a978d8`, HTML `ad17f18fb9629cf28917feb36205d77399953fdf52c076f1ac1a78bde204007e`; one line of each page changed |

## Permission check covers handler-discovered operations, ticket #144

Ticket #144 of spec #115, the permission twin of #116's PR #143 fix. `AddPouryaKitPermissions` registered `PermissionAuthorizer<T>` only for the attributed commands and queries the scanned assemblies declare, while `AddPouryaKitCqrs` also dispatches one a scanned handler handles that another assembly declares, such as a Module's Contracts. It now registers over `OperationTypes.Dispatchable`, the one discovery `AddPouryaKitCqrs` and `AddPouryaKitTenancy` read, made public: the Kit's one `InternalsVisibleTo` between packages is recorded as opening Cqrs for a trusted restore only. Environment: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.402, Docker 29.8.2, Archify 3.0.1 with Playwright's Chromium headless.

| Item | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Every dispatchable attributed operation is permission-checked | `RevokeAccessCommand`, declared with `[RequiresPermission]` in `PouryaKit.Security.Tests.Contracts` and handled by an internal handler in `PouryaKit.Security.Tests.Module`, both registered through `AddPouryaKitPermissions(module)` and `AddPouryaKitCqrs(module)`: an Actor without the permission gets `PermissionErrors.Denied` and the handler never runs; with it the handler runs and the command succeeds | `RegistrationTests.A_command_declared_in_another_assembly_and_handled_in_the_scanned_one_is_permission_checked` (`PouryaKit.Security.Tests`). Red once with the registration narrowed back to the operations the assemblies declare: `Assert.Single() Failure: The collection was empty` for the Actor without the permission |
| The refusal of a misplaced attribute covers the same set (review F3) | An attributed abstract, generic or non-operation type the assemblies declare still fails registration, and so does an attributed abstract command another assembly declares that a handler in the assemblies handles (`AbstractContractCommand`, handled in the test assembly): such a handler is accepted by `AddPouryaKitCqrs`, so the handler-discovered set is not concrete by construction | `RegistrationTests.A_permission_declared_on_a_type_that_is_not_one_concrete_command_or_query_fails_registration`. Red once with the check narrowed back to the declared types: `Assert.Equal() Failure: Collections differ at index 1`, the Contracts type missing |
| Docs | The skill's [security.md](../.agents/skills/pourya-kit/references/security.md) and [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md) (the permissions paragraph, Operation classification and the tenant requirement) say the set includes operations a handler in the assemblies handles. The execution-core diagram's Security range moved to the discovery (lines 35 to 40, "Dispatchable over ImplementingTypes") and pins `aebdeaf1fc92ba6b0c9fdf6e33085a6d53018a14`, the review-fix commit, refinalized: `ok true, status pass`, every gate passing, one line of the page changed | Source SHA-256 `679b4d48506f3adb67aeed92657ac61378b7392e5bd7a5776148dfa5d5ba61e5`, HTML `9b46e188920f9017e1ced398641ae74f782a3e7e422058ffb11c0930019398b2` |

## Tenant requirement composition check, ticket #145

Ticket #145 of spec #115, the owner's decision of 2026-10-16 on PR #143's Codex P1. `AddPouryaKitTenancy` registers the tenant requirement only for the assemblies it is given, so a Module whose `AddPouryaKitCqrs` scanned another assembly too composed with unguarded operations. Cqrs's internal `TenantRequirementCheck`, a composition check registered with the dispatcher, now fails the Host when it is built for each command or query a tenant-scoped Module's, or the tenant-scoped application's, recorded Feature assemblies make dispatchable (`HostComposition.FeatureAssembliesOf`, `OperationTypes.Dispatchable`) with no `TenantRequirement<T>` registered for it. `AddPouryaKitTenancy` is unchanged. Environment: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.402, Docker 29.8.2.

| Criterion | Test or change | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A tenant-scoped Module whose Cqrs scan covers an assembly its tenancy declaration did not name fails the Host at build, naming the Module, the operation and the fix | `PartlyTenantScopedModule` names `ModularMonolith.Tests.Unversioned` to `AddPouryaKitTenancy` and scans it and `ModularMonolith.Tests.Unowned` with `AddPouryaKitCqrs`: the build fails with exactly the one line naming the Module, `UnownedCommand` and the assembly to name to `AddPouryaKitTenancy`, though the Module registers another authorizer of `UnownedCommand` (review F1: a check satisfied by any authorizer would pass). The application's form names "The application" and the Host's own services | `CompositionCheckTests.A_tenant_scoped_module_that_dispatches_an_operation_its_tenancy_declaration_did_not_name_fails_the_host_when_built` and `A_tenant_scoped_application_that_dispatches_an_operation_its_tenancy_declaration_did_not_name_fails_the_host_when_built`: both failed with the check's registration switched off, and the Module's failed with the check weakened to accept any authorizer; both pass with the check as built |
| The sample, both templates and every existing test still compose | Catalog, in the sample and both templates, names its own assembly to both registrations | `ModularMonolith.Tests`: 647 passed. `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests`: 50 passed. `python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since 1e0b68c3` on `95d403f4` (the review fix; the runs on `e95d8b20` and `49ecfe5a` passed the same): `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` |

## Tenancy at save and delivery, ticket #117

Ticket #117 of spec #115: Multi-tenancy at the save and delivery seams, as registrations in existing packages. The EF Unit of Work's prepare step refuses with `TenantErrors.Mismatch` (`Tenant.Mismatch`, forbidden) a save of an `ITenantScoped` entity that is not the Operation's tenant's; the delivery step refuses a delivery with no tenant into a tenant-scoped Module before its Receipt; `RecurringCommandTenancyCheck` and `SnapshotKeyTenancyCheck` refuse a recurring command, and a Snapshot of an aggregate whose key two tenants could share, in such a Module; the Receipt and Snapshot version keys stay as built (T1, ADR 0006's #117 amendment). Inventory keys its copies of Catalog's products by tenant and product. Environment: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.402, Docker 29.8.2, EF Core 10.0.12, Npgsql EF Core 10.0.3, WolverineFx 6.48.3. Each guarantee was run once with its behaviour switched off, through a scratchpad script that changed one source, ran the class and restored the file.

| Criterion | Test or change | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A cross-tenant load through the set-aside filter fails at save whether the root, a child, a moved child or a cascaded part changed | The Stub's tenant-scoped `Binder`, with sheets on a required key, loaded with `TenantFilter` set aside: north's Operation renames south's binder, rewrites its sheet, moves its sheet into a north binder, moves a north sheet into it, and removes it outright with its sheets; each fails with `Tenant.Mismatch` and south's rows are unchanged, while the same change to north's own binders saves | `TenantOwnershipTests.A_change_to_another_tenants_binder_loaded_with_the_tenant_filter_set_aside_fails_forbidden_and_saves_nothing` (five cases). Red with the check off: all five. Red with only the changed entries judged, not the aggregates a change is attributed to: the sheet, and both moves |
| A cross-tenant load, or a tenant reassignment, followed by an update or a soft deletion | Catalog's `Product`, in a tenant-scoped Module: north's Operation loads south's product with the filter set aside, gives it north, then renames it or removes it (kept marked deleted); and gives its own product to south, then renames or removes it. Each fails with `Tenant.Mismatch`; the row keeps its tenant, name, `is_deleted` and version | `TenantOwnershipTests.A_product_whose_tenant_an_operation_changed_fails_forbidden_whether_then_renamed_or_removed` (four cases). Red with the check off: all four |
| An added aggregate of another tenant | A binder added for south in north's Operation fails with `Tenant.Mismatch` and is not saved; one added for north is | `TenantOwnershipTests.An_aggregate_added_for_another_tenant_than_the_operations_fails_forbidden_and_saves_nothing`. Red with the check off |
| A Notification, Integration Event or Snapshot delivered with no tenant into a tenant-scoped Module writes nothing and is retried then dead-lettered naming the reason | Catalog's own `ProductCreatedDomainEvent` to its `publish-product-created` subscription, and another Host's `TenantProbeV1` and `TenantProbeSnapshotV1`, which the tests' Host receives into Catalog with a handler that writes with plain SQL only, each sent to its subscription's queue with the Kit's headers and no tenant: one dead letter, `Tenant.Required (This operation runs only for a tenant, and its context names none.)` in its message, three attempts, no Receipt, nothing written. ## Dapper tenant rule, ticket #118

Ticket #118 of spec #115: the Dapper part of Multi-tenancy, a structure rule with no runtime seam. `ModuleRules.ReadsBeyondTheTenant` reads every string literal of a Module the Host records as tenant-scoped (`HostComposition.IsTenantScoped`, from the Host `Sample` builds and never starts) and holds each one that reads a table an `ITenantScoped` entity maps to one shape: one `SELECT` with no set operator, no subquery and no top-level `OR` in its `WHERE`, whose `WHERE` has a top-level conjunct `<alias>.tenant = @Tenant` for each such table it reads, or `tenant = @Tenant` when its `FROM` reads that one table. Every other shape is rejected. After the second review it reads the literal's tokens (`Sql.Tokens`) against one allow-list grammar. It reuses `Il` and `Sql` and adds no package. Catalog's `GetProduct` and `ListProducts` keep to the shape as written in #116 and are unchanged. Environment: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.402, Docker 29.8.2. Each red run below changed one or two sources through a scratchpad script that ran the class and restored the files.

| Item | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| The rule rejects a top-level `OR`, a `UNION` and an unfiltered join | `Offending.CrossTenantReadingQueryHandler` reads Catalog's tables three times, each naming `tenant = @Tenant`: inside a top-level `OR`, in one branch of a `UNION`, and for `catalog.products` of a join that leaves `catalog.categories c` unfiltered. Each is one violation naming its fault: `has an OR at the top level of its WHERE`, `has the set operator union`, `does not hold catalog.categories c to @Tenant with a top-level conjunct of its WHERE` | `DapperTenantTests.A_top_level_or_a_union_and_an_unfiltered_join_are_rejected_though_each_names_the_tenant`. Red with each weaker predicate: `tenant = @Tenant` anywhere in the text accepted (all three passed, and 13 shape cases); `OR` split as `AND` (the `OR` read passed); only the text before a set operator judged (the `UNION` read passed); one tenant-scoped table of a read held to the tenant enough (the join passed) |
| The rule accepts the sample's reads and judges them | Catalog is the one tenant-scoped Module, `catalog.categories` and `catalog.products` its tenant-scoped tables, column `tenant`; no violation, and the literals judged are exactly `GetProduct`'s and `ListProducts`' `SELECT` and `COUNT` | `DapperTenantTests.Every_sql_read_of_a_tenant_scoped_module_holds_each_tenant_scoped_table_to_the_tenant`, `DapperTenantTests.The_tenant_scoped_modules_come_from_the_host_and_their_tables_from_each_modules_model` |
| The shape, case by case | Accepted with an `OR` in parentheses, a join holding both tables, `between ... and`, quoted names, a comment, a table no `ITenantScoped` entity maps, and a `delete from` (the write rule's); rejected for an `OR` outside parentheses, `@TenantId`, `not p.tenant = @Tenant`, the table's name where it has an alias, a comma join, an unqualified predicate over two tables, an `and` a `between` takes, no `WHERE`, a subquery, `intersect`, a CTE, a fragment, an `insert ... select` and two statements; after the review, also a `CASE` at the top level of the `WHERE`, a dollar-quoted block, a `FROM` item in parentheses, and an `OR` after a column named `"limit"`, 25 cases in all | `DapperTenantTests.A_read_of_a_tenant_scoped_table_is_held_to_one_shape` |
| The dual review's fix round (F1 to F5) | The rule rejects what it does not parse instead of reading further: a `CASE` at the top level of the `WHERE` (F1: its `and` was split as a conjunct), a dollar-quoted block (F2: its body was read as the predicate), a `FROM` item in parentheses (F3: its relations were never read); keywords inside double-quoted identifiers end no clause (F4: `"limit"` hid a top-level `OR`). The skill's two sentences say the table of a `delete from` is the SQL-write rule's (F5) | Each of the four new shape cases, run against the reviewed reader (`ModuleRules.cs` and `Sql.cs` of `b1411303`), failed with `Actual: null`: the reader accepted it |
| The second review's fix round (F6 to F9) | The reader no longer finds reads by context and rejects the shapes it recognises; it tokenises the literal and admits only the grammar `SELECT list FROM item ((JOIN \| INNER JOIN \| LEFT JOIN) item ON condition \| , item)* WHERE conjunct (AND conjunct)* [ORDER BY list] [LIMIT @p \| n] [OFFSET @p \| n]`, each item `[schema.]table [[AS] alias]`. A literal reads a tenant-scoped table when it names one anywhere and holds a SQL command word, `from` or `join`, so `TABLE catalog.products` is read and rejected as no `SELECT` (F7); a parameter is a token, so `@limit` ends no clause and the `OR` after it is found (F6); a block comment is rejected, a nested one included (F8), and `Sql.Code` now nests block comments as PostgreSQL does for the SQL-write and transaction-control rules; a column-alias list after an item is rejected (F9). Catalog's literals that name a table without SQL (`"/products"`, `"catalog.products.remove"`, the cache tag `"products"`) stay outside the rule | Each of the four cases, run against the reviewed reader (`ModuleRules.cs` and `Sql.cs` of `b72a6961`), failed with `Actual: null`. `DapperWriteTests.Sql_writes_are_read_by_the_table_they_name` gains `/* a /* b */ ' */ delete from catalog.products --'`, red with the comment closed at its first `*/` (no table found). `DapperTenantTests.A_read_of_a_tenant_scoped_table_is_held_to_one_shape`: 32 cases |
| Each Dapper handler of the tenant-scoped Module returns only the Operation's tenant's rows, at the Gateway with two tenants | Covered by #116's tests, which send `GetProductQuery` and `ListProductsQuery`, the two Dapper handlers, for two tenants; nothing added | `TenancyTests.A_tenant_reads_writes_and_removes_none_of_another_tenants_products_through_the_gateway` and `TenancyTests.Over_http_a_tenant_gets_and_lists_none_of_another_tenants_products`.
